## 1. Background information on the procedure

## 1.1. Type II variation

Pursuant to Article 16 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 1234/2008, Daiichi Sankyo Europe GmbH submitted to the European Medicines Agency on 03 December 2025 an application for a variation.

The following variation was requested:

| Variation requested   | Variation requested                                                         | Type              |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------|
| C.I.6.a               | Addition of a new therapeutic indication or modification of an approved one | Variation type II |

Extension of indication to include, as monotherapy, the first-line treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who are not candidates for PD1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy for DATROWAY, based on final results from study D926PC00001 (TROPION-Breast02). This is a Phase 3, randomised, open-label, 2 arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with investigator's choice chemotherapy in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated in accordance. Version 1.1 of the RMP has also been submitted. In addition, the MAH took the opportunity to introduce minor changes to the PI. As part of the application, the MAH is requesting a 1-year extension of the market protection.

## Information on paediatric requirements

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No 1901/2006, the application included (an) EMA Decision(s) P/0279/2021 on the granting of a (product-specific) waiver for the treatment of breast cancer.

## Information relating to orphan market exclusivity

## Similarity

Pursuant to Article 8 of Regulation (EC) No. 141/2000 and Article 3 of Commission Regulation (EC) No 847/2000, the MAH did not submit a critical report addressing the possible similarity with authorised orphan medicinal products because there is no authorised orphan medicinal product for a condition related to the proposed indication.

## MAH request for additional market protection

The MAH requested consideration of its application in accordance with Article 14(11) of Regulation (EC) 726/2004 - one year of market protection for a new indication.

## Scientific advice

The MAH Scientific Advice from the CHMP on December 2021 (EMA/SA/0000070789). The Scientific Advice pertained to clinical aspects of the dossier.

## 2. Scientific discussion

## 2.1. Introduction

## 2.1.1. Problem statement

## Disease or condition

Datroway (datopotamab deruxtecan) as monotherapy is indicated for the first-line treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy (see SmPC section 5.1).

## Epidemiology and risk factors

Breast cancer (BC) is one of the most common cancers globally, with approximately 2.3 million new cases in 2022 (11.6% of all new cancers) and an estimated 665,000 deaths, making it the fourth leading cause of cancer-related fatalities (Bray et al 2024). In Europe there were about 605,000 new diagnoses and 160,000 deaths due to breast cancer reported in 2023 (ECIS 2023).

Despite advances in the diagnosis and treatment of BC, around 5% to 10% of women diagnosed with BC have metastatic disease at the time of diagnosis, and up to 30% of women with earlystage non-metastatic BC will develop metastatic disease (Cardoso et al 2018, O'Shaughnessy 2005, OECD 2021).

Risk factors of TNBC include family history, longer duration of oral contraceptive use and higher breast density whereas later age at menarche, later age at first birth and breastfeeding are associated with decreased risk of TNBC.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Biologic features

The targeted disease of Triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) is biologically heterogeneous and should be interpreted as an operational term rather than a fixed pathobiological entity. Previously these patients could only be treated with chemotherapy because of the absence or low expression of oestrogen receptor (&gt;1%), progesterone receptor (&gt;1%), and HER2 expression. In fact, approximately 35% of TNBCs have been reported to be HER2 low illustrating a pivotal difference between triple negative tumours and \"truly\" triple negative tumours and highlighting just one of several pathobiological entities within the TNBC classification. (Darekshan et al 2022)

Clinical practice classifies 3 BC subtypes, based on molecular characteristics: HER2-positive, HRpositive but HER2-negative and TNBC. Approximately 70% of all BCs are of the subtype HRpositive, HER2-negative (Howlader et al 2014). TNBC, characterized by the absence of ER, progesterone receptor and low or no HER2 protein expression, accounts for 10% to 20% of new BC diagnoses (Allison et al 2020, Wolff et al 2018, Dixon-Douglas et al 2022).

TNBC is typically more aggressive than hormone-receptor positive disease with poorer prognosis due to higher histological grades and the lack of actionable treatment targets, except in patients with PD-L1-positive disease or BRCA mutations (Bonotto et al 2014). Among patients diagnosed with metastatic TNBC, between 25.5% and 37.5% have de novo metastatic disease (Stage IV at initial diagnosis). In patients initially diagnosed with non-metastatic TNBC, up to 40% to 50% experience recurrence, primarily as distant metastases, following standard treatment (Costa and Gradishar 2017, Stewart et al 2019, Jwa et al 2016, Rugo et al 2021).

## Clinical presentation, diagnosis and stage/prognosis

TNBC typically presents as a rapidly enlarging, palpable breast mass in younger women and is frequently high-grade with early nodal involvement; diagnosis is established through diagnostic imaging followed by core needle biopsy demonstrating absence of ER, PR, and HER2 expression on immunohistochemistry, with MRI and genetic testing (notably BRCA1/2) often used for disease characterization. Prognosis is strongly stage-dependent with stage I disease showing favourable outcomes and 5-year survival exceeding 85%, stage II disease showing increased recurrence risk but improved survival in patients achieving pathologic complete response after neoadjuvant chemotherapy, stage III disease associated with high rates of locoregional and distant relapse despite multimodal treatment, and stage IV disease conferring poor prognosis with median survival typically under two years and common metastases to lung, liver, brain, and bone (Liedtke et al 2008, Howlader et al 2020).

## Management

## Current treatment

The choice of initial systemic therapy for metastatic TNBC is individualized based on a number of factors, including PD-L1 status, BRCA status, tumour burden, rate of disease progression, previous chemotherapy exposure, medical history and patient preference. Single-agent chemotherapy remains the mainstay of first-line treatment for metastatic or locally recurrent inoperable TNBC, with the addition of checkpoint inhibitors for patients with PD-L1 positive tumours, while PARP inhibitors may be considered for those patients with germline BRCA mutations (Gradishar et al 2024, Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024).

## A. Gennari et al.

## Annals of Oncology

e ESMO-MCBS v1.193 was used to calculate scores for new therapies/indications approved by the EMA or FDA. The scores have been calculated by the ESMO-MCBS Working Group and validated by the ESMO Guidelines Committee (https://www.esmo.org/guidelines/esmo-mcbs/scale-evaluation-forms-v1.0-v1.1/scale-evaluationforms-v1.1).

For first-line treatment of metastatic TNBC, chemotherapy remains the standard, particularly for the approximately 70% of patients who are not candidates for immunotherapy, which includes those patients with PD-L1-negative disease, comorbidities precluding PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitors, or recurrence after such therapy (Gradishar et al 2024, Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024, Punie et al 2025). In cases where immunotherapy is not an option, sequential single-agent chemotherapy is generally recommended (Gradishar et al 2024, Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024). The preferred first-line chemotherapy is a taxane or an anthracycline, with alternatives including capecitabine, gemcitabine, eribulin, vinorelbine, and platinum (Giordano et al 2018, Gradishar et al 2024, Cardoso et al 2024). Re-challenge with taxanes is recommended, provided that there has been at least 1 year of disease-free survival (Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024). The median PFS for patients treated with first-line chemotherapy for either recurrent or de novo metastatic TNBC is approximately 5.5 months, with median OS less than 2 years (Cortes et al 2020, Emens et al 2021, Miles et al 2021). With regard to disease-free interval (DFI) status, patients with DFI &lt; 12 months have worse outcomes, with median PFS less than 4 months and median OS approximately 10 months (Dent et al 2024).

In patients with germline BRCA mutations, about 20% of TNBC cases (Ye et al 2021; GonzalezAngulo et al 2011), PARP inhibitors are an alternative treatment option to chemotherapy based on results of the OlympiAD (NCT02000622) and EMBRACA (NCT01945775) studies (Gradishar et al

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

2024, Gennari et al 2021, Punie et al 2025, Robson et al 2017, Litton et al 2018). If a patient had a germline BRCA mutation, eligibility for the pivotal study TB02 was dependent on the investigator assessing that another therapy (such as PARP inhibitor) was not considered the best treatment option for the patient.

For patients with metastatic TNBC with PD-L1-positive tumours, anti-PD-1/PD-L1 agents combined with chemotherapy are recommended as first-line treatments based on clinical trial data (Gradishar et al 2024, Gennari et al 2021). Some patients with PD-L1-positive disease are not candidates for immunotherapy, for reasons including comorbidities that preclude PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy or relapse after prior PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy for early-stage BC.

Sacituzumab govitecan is an anti-TROP2 ADC recommended for the treatment of adult patients with metastatic TNBC, who have received 2 or more prior systemic therapies, at least one of them for metastatic disease, but it is currently not recommended in the first-line setting (Gennari et al 2021, Gradishar et al 2024).

Finally, patients with HER2 low or ultralow expressing tumours (IHC 1+ or IHC 2+/ISH-, or HER2ultralow tumour status, described as IHC 0 with membrane staining (IHC &gt;0&lt;1+)) now have the option of treatment with another ADC, trastuzumab deruxtecan, in the metastatic setting after having previously received chemotherapy after disease progression.

## Unmet medical need

Treatment options for first-line metastatic TNBC (whether recurrent or de novo) are limited to single-agent chemotherapy when patients have tumors that are BRCA wild-type and do not have imminent organ failure and for those who are not candidates for immunotherapy, which includes those patients with PD-L1-negative disease, comorbidities precluding PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitors, or recurrence after such therapy (Gradishar et al 2024; Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024, Punie et al 2025). However, single-agent chemotherapy results in poor survival outcomes and significant hematologic and gastrointestinal toxicity (Gradishar et al 2005, Miller et al 2007, Piccart-Gebhart et al 2008, Emens et al 2021, Cortes et al 2022, Brufsky et al 2012, Park et al 2019, Perez et al 2010, Pivot et al 2016). Despite advances in systemic therapies, the median overall survival for metastatic TNBC remains under 2 years, even in recent first-line clinical trials (Yao et al 2019, Kim et al 2021; Cortes et al 2022). Patients with relapse occurring within 12 months after completing chemotherapy for primary breast cancer experience rapid disease progression and extremely poor prognosis with a mOS shorter than 1 year (Dent et al 2024). Among patients with metastatic TNBC, those with brain metastases have the poorest prognosis with median OS ranging from 6 to 9 months (Yao et al 2019, Gao et al 2021, Zimmer 2021). Published real-world data indicate that only approximately 50% of patients who receive first-line TNBC treatment go on to receive secondline treatment (Punie et al 2025); therefore, the unmet need is especially pronounced for treatment options that demonstrate benefit in the first-line setting. Given the aggressive nature of metastatic TNBC and the limited efficacy of current treatments, particularly for those who are ineligible for immunotherapies, there is a need for innovative therapies that offer improved efficacy with a manageable and tolerable safety profile.

## 2.1.2. About the product

Datopotamab is a humanised anti-TROP2 IgG1 attached to deruxtecan, a topoisomerase I inhibitor (DXd) bound by a tetrapeptide-based cleavable linker. The antibody-drug conjugate binds to TROP2 expressed on the surface of certain tumour cells. After binding, datopotamab deruxtecan undergoes internalisation into the tumour cells and the subsequent release of DXd results in DNA damage and apoptotic cell death via topoisomerase I inhibition.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Datroway (datopotamab deruxtecan) is approved in the EU as monotherapy for the treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic HR-positive, HER2-negative breast cancer who have received endocrine therapy and at least one line of chemotherapy in the advanced setting based on the results of the Phase III TROPION-Breast01 (TB01) study.

Datroway should be prescribed by a physician and administered under the supervision of a healthcare professional experienced in the use of anticancer medicinal products.

The recommended dose is 6 mg/kg (up to a maximum of 540 mg for patients ≥90 kg) of body weight given as an intravenous infusion once every 3 weeks (21-day cycle) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.

## 2.1.3. The development programme/compliance with CHMP guidance/scientific advice

CHMP scientific advice concerning the development and study plan for the pivotal trial TB02 was given on December 2021 (EMA/SA/0000070789).

In summary, the CHMP advised the Sponsor to:

- Include anthracyclines as an additional comparator option, or inclusion criteria should clarify that prior therapy must have included an anthracycline.
- When feasible biopsy recurrent disease to confirm histology and to re-assess tumour biology (including ER, PgR, HER2).
- Include pre-planned analyses of choice of chemotherapy in the control arm as this was Investigator's choice.
- All efforts should be made to determine PD-L1 expression prior to study inclusion, to avoid an unknown PD-L1 status.
- Make all efforts to be able to conclude on the TROP2 expression-response correlation data (TROP2 negative, TROP2 low and TROP2 overexpression)
- Evaluate the germline BRCA 1/2 mutation status (gBRCAm) of all patients as PARP inhibitors are recommended as a treatment option for gBRCAm PD-L1 negative mTNBC.
- Include a pre-planned analysis for taxane vs non-taxane chemotherapy.
- include an anthracycline (monotherapy) as an option for patients.

CHMP scientific advice was generally followed, but not on all points. Several of these may impact the external generalizability of the trial data. Anthracycline monotherapy was not included in the Investigator's choice of chemotherapy (ICC) despite this being a standard practice referenced in the ESMO guidelines (Gennari et al, 2021). Here taxane or anthracycline monotherapy arerecommended as first choices to be used with the opposite agent used at progression. However, in the pivotal study TB02, only taxanes (omitting docetaxel) were permitted and no combination treatment was possible, such as nab-paclitaxel + carboplatin. While the treatment possibilities in the Investigator's Choice arm are recommended by the ESMO guideline, the omission of anthracyclines and combination chemotherapy leaves the possibility of at least some patients receiving an inferior treatment in the control arm, which also may be implied in the difference between withdrawals in the control arm (ICC) (31 patients [9.7%]) and Dato-DXd (8 patients [2.5%]) arms. This was justified on the basis that previous studies in the first-line setting such as IMpassion130, IMpassion131 and KEYNOTE-355 studies did not include an anthracycline

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

monotherapy option either. While this design choice limits external generalisability of the TB02 trial's results, it does not preclude efficacy demonstration in this procedural setting and it is considered acceptable given that the TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) trial is in line with the recent clinical trial context (i.e. IMpassion130, IMpassion131 and KEYNOTE-355 studies).

The pivotal trial required that all participants must have had a FFPE metastatic (excluding bone) or locally recurrent inoperable tumour sample available, collected ≤ 3 months prior to screening. If neither an adequate FFPE block nor the minimum of 20 slides were available from metastatic or locally recurrent inoperable tumour, or if a biopsy was not feasible for safety reasons, it was permitted to use an archival tumour specimen obtained before the diagnosis of locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic breast cancer.

## 2.1.4. General comments on compliance with GCP

## 2.2. The Sponsor's procedures, internal quality control measures, and audit programs provide reassurance that the Dato-DXd clinical development program is being conducted as per GCP.Non-clinical aspects

No new non-clinical data have been submitted in this application, which was considered acceptable by the CHMP.

## 2.2.1. Ecotoxicity/environmental risk assessment

The dose, indication and patient population in this procedure are covered by the indication considered for the refined PEC in the previously submitted ERA during the initial MA (see Datroway EPAR).

The concerned moiety in terms of ERA of datopotamab deruxtecan is deruxtecan (DXd), a topoisomerase I inhibitor. DXd is released from the mAb and linker portion upon binding to the target cell. Therefore, the environmental risk assessment considers this molecule in isolation. The rest of the molecule is of protein nature, which is susceptible to rapid degradation in the environment, and, in accordance with the guideline (CHMP EMEA/CHMP/SWP/4447/00), ERA studies are not required. The maximum daily dose of DXd for a European adult with an average weight of 70.8 kg (Walpole et al. 2012) is estimated to be 5.5 mg per inhabitant per day. The calculation assumes the maximum daily dose is taken every day by all patients.

## 2.2.2. Conclusion on the non-clinical aspects

The updated data submitted in this application do not lead to a significant increase in environmental exposure further to the use of datopotamab deruxtecan.

Considering the above data, datopotamab deruxtecan is not expected to pose a risk to the environment

## 2.3.2. Pharmacokinetics

Dato-DXd and total anti-TROP2 antibody (total Dato Ab) in plasma were measured by a validated Gyrolab platform-based LBA with fluorescent detection. DXd (ADC payload) in plasma was measured by validated LC-MS/MS method.

## Immunogenicity

Anti-Dato-DXd antibodies were measured by a validated MSD platform-based LBA with ECL detection. A cell-based method was used for detection of neutralising antibody (Nab).

## Pop PK modelling

Population PK analysis was implemented by NONMEM, version 7.3.0 (Icon Development Solutions). The FOCE inter method was primarily used for NONMEM computations. R, version 4.1.0 and above and the R packages Xpose as well as Perl-speaks-NONMEM, version 4.2.0 were used for exploratory analysis, executing NONMEM runs and post-processing of NONMEM output.

A previous Pop PK model for Dato-DXd and DXd, based on data from the TROPION-Breast01 submission (TL01, TL05, TP01, TB01), was updated to include data from two new clinical studies. TROPION-PanTumour02 (TP02) was a multiple-cohort study of Dato-DXd in Chinese patients with various cancer types including triple-negative breast cancer. TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) was a randomised study in patients not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy in first-line (FL) locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer.

TP02 (N=119) and TB02 (N=319) contributed with 3359 Dato-DXd and 3307 DXd additional observations, of which 459 Dato-DXd and 442 DXd were BLQ and excluded. Most BLQ samples were taken pre-first dose. There were no outliers or duplicates identified in the additional data.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 1 Model Schematic

<!-- image -->

The DatoDXd model was a two-compartmental distribution model with parallel CLlinDatoDXd and CLnonlinDatoDXd from central compartment. The DXd model was a one-compartment model with CLDXd from the central compartment. The release of DXd from the intact Dato-DXd was equal to the total (linear + nonlinear) elimination rate of Dato-DXd. The payload:intact ADC ratio (PIR) was decreasing with time both within the dosing cycle and between cycles (cycle 1 vs later cycles). IIV was characterised on CLlinDatoDXd, VCDatoDXd, VpDatoDXd, QDatoDXd, Vmax, CLDXd, and VcDXd. The residual error was described by proportional RUV (implemented as additive on the logscale) with an exponential IIV term.

Impact of covariates were re-evaluated except for body weight which was included on all parameters with estimated IIV, except for Q and Vmax for Dato-DXd. The final updated model also included the covariate-parameter relationships of ALB, age, region, sex and tumour type on CLlinDatoDXd; sex on VcDatoDXd and VcDXd; baseline tumour size on Vmax; ALB, AST, TBIL, and region on CLDXd. All covariate relationships included in the previous model were statistically significant in this analysis, with the addition of tumour type as a covariate for CLlinDatoDXd.

The parameter estimates for the updated Dato-DXd model and DXd model are shown in the below tables.

Individual exposure metrics have been derived from the individual empirical Bayes estimates (EBEs) as drivers for exposure response analysis related to safety and efficacy.

## Exposure-response modelling

Exposure metrics for efficacy, Dato-DXd AUCC1, and safety analyses, Dato-DXd and DXd AUCC1, were derived from the updated Pop PK model. Dataset preparation and statistical analysis were performed using SAS version 9.4 and R, Version 4.0.2 or higher.

## Efficacy

Exposure-efficacy analysis was performed on data from TNBC patients in study TB02 (Dato-DXd arm: N=319; ICC arm: N=321) with endpoints: progression free survival (PFS) and overall survival (OS). The maturity of the PFS and OS were 63% and 54%, respectively, at the data cut off (DCO) 25 August 2025.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

Kaplan-Meier curves were used for initial analyses with log-rank testing. Time-to event analyses were conducted by Cox proportional hazards modelling. Model fitting was done using Maximum Likelihood Estimation. Prior to model fitting continuous covariates were centred at approximate median values. Influence of covariates was tested using a stepwise approach with forward inclusion (p&lt;0.01) followed by backward elimination (p&lt;0.001).

## PFS

AUCC1 Dato-DXd was not statistically associated with PFS and no further covariates were evaluated.

Table 8 Cox proportional hazard model for PFS

| Code         | Label           |   Hazard Ratio |   Lower 95% CI |   Upper 95% CI |   Wald's nominal p- value |
|--------------|-----------------|----------------|----------------|----------------|---------------------------|
| AUC1_DatoDXd | AUC C1 Dato-DXD |          0.999 |          0.998 |              1 |                     0.151 |

Cox proportional hazards regression analysis with area under the curve (AUC) as a continuous exposure variable. p-values are derived from the Wald test for the coefficient of the AUC term in the Cox model. Each unit increase in AUC1\\_DatoDXd (μg·day/mL) multiplies the hazard function by 0.999,

The PFS model diagnostics reported a Schoenfeld p-value of 0.0018 with a correlation between Schoenfeld residuals and time of 0.17. A VPC of model predicted PFS compared to observed is shown in the figure below.

## OS

In the univariate OS model AUCC1 Dato-DXd was associated with lower OS (table below).

Table 9 Summary of Hazard Ratio for the Univariate OS Model

| Code         | Label            |   Hazard Ratio |   Lower 95% CI |   Upper 95% CI |   Wald’s nominal P-value |
|--------------|------------------|----------------|----------------|----------------|--------------------------|
| AUC1_DatoDXd | AUC C1 Dato- DXD |          0.999 |          0.997 |              1 |                    0.029 |

Cox proportional hazards regression analysis with area under the curve (AUC) as a continuous exposure variable. p-values are derived from the Wald test for the coefficient of the AUC term in the Cox model. Each unit increase in AUC1\\_DatoDXd (μg·day/mL) multiplies the hazard function by 0.999.

In a covariate screening confounding risk factors such as higher baseline tumor size, lower baseline albumin and prior DFI were identified as significant covariates for OS.

## Safety

Exposure-safety analysis was performed on data from an all-solid-tumour pool (studies TP01, TP02, TB01, TB02, TL01, TL05; N=1527) and a breast cancer pool (studies TP01, TP02, TB01 and TB02; N=843) with endpoints: Grade 3 or higher treatment emergent adverse events (TEAEs), TEAEs leading to dose reduction, and adverse events of special interest (AESI). Patients were classified into four groups based on exposure quartiles.

Exploratory analysis of exposure and safety events in both data pools were initially depicted in boxplots. Probability of occurrence of key safety endpoints and AESIs were modelled using DatoDXd Cycle 1 AUC and DXd Cycle 1 AUC as continuous exposure metrics in logistic regression. A

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

covariate search within the breast and all solid-tumour cancer pool was subsequently performed using the logistic regression model if an exposure - AE endpoints relationship was identified. Influence of covariates were tested using the stepwise approach with forward inclusion (p&lt;0.01) followed by backward elimination (p&lt;0.001). Model comparison was performed by means of AIC (Akaike Information Criterion). The modelled exposure-safety relations were quantified in Forest plots across exposure quantiles.

## Tumour growth inhibition model

A tumour growth inhibition (TGI) model was developed for describing the tumour size data from patients with triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who had received Dato-DXd at 6 or 8 mg/kg from J101 study (N = 40, Data cutoff: 22nd July 2022) or received Dato-DXd at 6 mg/kg from TP02 (N = 76, Data cutoff : 6th May 2024). A predominate part of patients originated from China (65.5%). Body weights ranged from 38 to 124 kg. Of note, no dose-capping was applied.

The analysis was performed using a SAEM approach using Monolix 2021R1 (Lixoft, France). R studio (version 4.1.3) was used in the analysis for pre- and post-processing of data and model output. Observed tumour size data is shown in the below figure.

TP02 (N = 76)J101 (N = 40)

<!-- image -->

(A) longitudinal profiles of absolute tumour size; (B) longitudinal profiles of tumour size compared to baseline (%). Horizontal dash line represents baseline (100%), i.e., a 0% tumour change. Each point represents an observation, and each solid line represents one patient.

Maximum tumour reduction was greater in patients who had higher Dato-DXd PK exposure. Patients with median Dato-DXd AUC1 at 739 ug.d/mL achieved 5% greater tumour reduction at nadir (i.e., greatest shrinkage observed) compared to the ones at 574 ug.d/mL/mL (below figure).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 8 Maximum tumor shrinkage to baseline by Dato-DXd AUC1

<!-- image -->

Individual PK profiles and PK exposure metrics were predicted based on PopPK model EBE method. Evaluated PK exposure metrics in the TGI model included Cycle 1 Dato-DXd AUC (AUC1), Cycle 1 Dato-DXd Ctrough (Ctrough1), and time-varying (i.e., cycle-specific) Dato-DXd AUC.

Tumour growth inhibition was evaluated in an exposure-dependent manner. Different tumour growth models and tumour inhibition models were evaluated. Dato-DXd AUC1 to drive the tumor killing function significantly improved model performance.

The final model was parameterised by the initial tumour size (TS0), an exponential tumour growth function (kge), a linear tumour killing function (kkill) and a correlation term. IIV was evaluated on all parameters and residuals described by a proportional error function. Table 17 shows the parameter estimates of the final TGI model.

## Pharmacokinetic in the target population

## Study Design

TROPION-Breast02 is a Phase III, randomised, open-label, 2-arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with ICC in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

## Pharmacokinetic Results

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The two comparable drug products, to-be-marketed Lyo-DP and clinical Lyo-DP, was used in the TB02 study. A total of 319 participants from the Dato-DXd arm were included in the PK analysis set. No formal non-compartmental analysis was conducted due to the sparse PK sampling scheme in this study. After repeated IV administration of Dato-DXd 6 mg/kg Q3W, minimal accumulation was observed. Steady-state concentrations of Dato-DXd, anti-TROP2 antibody, and DXd were generally reached by Cycle 4 and maintained thereafter. For the summary of plasma concentrations of Dato-DXd, anti-TROP2 antibody, and DXd by visits/timepoints, see below table.

## Impact of anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) on PK

The geometric mean peak and trough plasma concentrations of Dato-DXd were comparable among TE-ADA-positive, nAb-positive, and ADA-negative participants across all time points, and within the variabilities of data observed in ADA-negative participants. Overall, there was no apparent effect of ADA or nAb on the PK of Dato-DXd.

## Special populations

On the basis of the updated PopPK model (see above) the effect of relevant covariates on the DatoDXd exposures at Cycle 3 (AUC3 and Cmax3) at 6.0 mg/kg administered on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle were re-evaluated.

The impact of baseline albumin, age, sex, region, baseline tumour size, hepatic impairment, renal impairment, tumour type, TROP2 expression and treatment emergent ADA on Dato-DXd and DXd PK exposure (first-cycle and third-cycle Dato-DXd and DXd AUC and Cmax) were not deemed to be clinically relevant, as the impacts were generally within the 80% to 125% range therefore no dose adjustment is needed based on baseline patient characteristics. Body weight was identified as a clinically relevant covariate. Participants with body weight ≥ 90 kg are recommended to receive a flat dose of 540 mg.

## 2.3.3. Pharmacodynamics

## Mechanism of action

Dato-DXd is a TROP2-targeted antibody and DNA topoisomerase I inhibitor conjugate. The antiTROP2 component is a humanised IgG1κ mAB. The total anti-TROP2 antibody is the sum of all DXd-conjugated and unconjugated mAb. The payload, DXd, is a DNA topoisomerase I inhibitor derivative of exatecan - the active metabolite of irinotecan. The mAb is covalently conjugated to a drug-linker, MAAA-1162a, which is composed of a cleavable maleimide tetrapeptide linker and the payload (DXd).

Dato-DXd binds to TROP2, and, after cell internalisation, the payload is released from the druglinker through enzymatic processing. The released drug inhibits topoisomerase I, which leads to the inhibition of cell replication and promotes apoptosis of the target tumour cells. The released drug is cell membrane-permeable, giving it the ability to penetrate and act in surrounding cancer cells. It has been reported that ADCs with DXd can exhibit a bystander effect, where the payload can diffuse out of a targeted cell into adjacent cells (Ogitani et al 2016).

## 2.3.4. PK/PD modelling

## Exposure-response models

The analysis of efficacy endpoints encompassed all TNBC participants in the PK analysis set (n=319) from TB02. The exposure metrics for all participants included in the exposure efficacy and exposure safety datasets were derived from the popPK model of Dato-DXd using individual empirical Bayes estimates. Models were performed on AUC in cycle 1.

## Exposure-safety models

The exposure-safety analysis was performed in two pools: all-solid-tumour pool and the BC only pool.

- A total of 1527 participants were included in the all-solid-tumour pool: 4 at 0.27 mg/kg, 5 at 0.5 mg/kg, 7 at 1 mg/kg, 6 at 2 mg/kg, 50 at 4 mg/kg, 1365 at 6 mg/kg, 82 at 8 mg/kg and 8 at 10 mg/kg.
- A total of 843 participants were included in the BC only pool: 841 at 6 mg/kg, and 2 at 8 mg/kg.

AUC1 of Dato-DXd and DXd are used as the exposure metrics for all safety endpoints. Logistic regression model was used to derive the exposure-safety relationship.

Treatment-induced ADA+, defined as ADA positive post-baseline and not detected at baseline (negative or missing).

Treatment-boosted ADA+, defined as a baseline positive ADA titer that was boosted to a 4-fold or higher level following drug administration.

TE-ADA+ are patients who were either treatment-boosted ADA+ or treatment-induced ADA+.

Non-TE-ADA+ are patients who are ADA positive but not fulfilling the conditions for TE-ADA+.

ADA- is defined as patients who were ADA- at all assessments, including baseline and post-baseline.

nAb+ is defined as patients with positive nAb assessment at any visit.

TE persistently ADA+, defined as being TE-ADA+ and having at least 2 post-baseline ADA+ measurements with ≥ 16 weeks between first and last positive, post-baseline (Not applicable for TL01, TP01, TP02, and TL05).

TE-nAb+ (nAb incidence), defined as TE-ADA+ patients with a positive nAb assessment.

If a patient had more than one titer result, the maximum post-baseline titer result was used, except for the category ADA+ at baseline only'.

## 2.3.5. Discussion on clinical pharmacology

In this variation, the MAH is applying for use of the antibody-drug conjugate Datroway (datopotamab deruxtecan, Dato-DXd) as monotherapy for first-line treatment of adult patient with unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who are not candidates for PD-

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy. The clinical pharmacology of Dato-DXd has been previously characterised and presented in the initial approval in BC (EPAR EMA/CHMP/78522/2025) and is reflected in the current product information.

The current application is primarily based on the pivotal study TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) in TNBC patients. The study's dosing regimen, 6 mg/kg IV infusion once every 3 weeks (21-day cycle), was the same as in approved BC indication of Datroway, which was based on the pivotal study TB01. Dato-Dxd, total anti-trop-2 antibody (total Ab) and Dxd in plasma were quantified with the validated methods applied in the initial Datroway MA. The conducted bioanalysis in TB02 study was in accordance with regulatory requirements. Pharmacokinetics in TB02 study was investigated using sparse PK-sampling. Overall, exposure of Dato-DXd and DXd, on basis of mean Ceoi (end of infusion) and Cpre-dose plasma concentrations values, were similar in TNBC patients as in BC patients in study TB01. Variability was also comparable.

In conclusion, the pharmacokinetics of Dato-DXd and DXd in TNBC patients in TB02 study is considered as supportive for the recommended dosing regimen. There was no apparent effect on ADAs on Dato-DXd PK, consistent with previous conclusions. An analysis of intrinsic and extrinsic factors utilizing the updated PopPK model was in line with previous investigations and these are adequately reported in SmPC.

A previous Pop PK model developed for Dato-DXd and DXd was re-estimated after addition of the sparse data from two clinical studies, TROPION-PanTumour02 (TP-02) and TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) where patients had received Dato-DXd IV 6 mg/kg Q3W. The updated Pop PK model was used to derive individual exposure metrics based on empirical Bayes estimates for evaluation of PK in patients with TNBC in special populations and for exposure-response analyses. Overall the updated Pop PK model is considered adequate for support of PK and for E-R analyses in TNBC patients. Of note, the clinical studies were conducted without a dose cap for individuals of ≥90 kg, applied to reduce occurrence of safety issues such as ocular surface events shown to be exposure dependent. Body weights ranged from 38 to 99.6 kg in TP-02 and 36 to 150 kg in TB02. Dato-DXd and DXd exposure with and without a dose cap was predicted and indicated individuals with a body weight ≥90 kg and dose-cap (fixed 540 mg) would achieve exposures in the same range as individuals with a body weight &lt;90 kg who received 6 mg/kg.

The mechanism of action is that datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) binds to TROP2, and, after cell internalisation, the payload is released from the drug-linker through enzymatic processing. The released drug inhibits topoisomerase I, which leads to the inhibition of cell replication and promotes apoptosis of the target tumour cells. The released drug is cell membrane-permeable, giving it the ability to penetrate and act in surrounding cancer cells so that DXd can exhibit a bystander effect, where the payload can diffuse out of a targeted cell into adjacent cells (Ogitani et al 2016).

No new studies were conducted that significantly informs primary or secondary pharmacology in this extension of indication. However, new dose-response analyses for efficacy and safety were conducted based on the TB02 trial.

Although no significant associations were identified in exposure-efficacy analyses (PFS or OS) at Cycle 1 Dato-DXd AUC, numerical differences between lower and upper AUC1 quartiles for PFS and OS could suggest that efficacy in the lowest quartile (Q1) is lower than for the other quartiles (Q24). For Q2 to Q4, median PFS was 403 days, 396 days and 361 days, respectively, whereas the median PFS for Q1 was 210 days. Per quartile median OS was 772 days, 817 days and 720 days for Q2-4 with a shorter OS for Q1 (508 days). Additional p-value of response relationship remained below the significance threshold with a p-value of 0.023. Furthermore, Cox Proportional Hazard

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

modelling showed the OS difference is less attributed to low exposure in Cycle 1 but suggested important influence by confounding risk factors such as higher baseline tumor size, lower baseline albumin and prior DFI which were identified as significant covariates (p &lt; 0.005) for OS. Thus, it can be concluded that there is no dose-efficacy relationship for Dato-DXd. It is considered that in this dataset an unbalanced distribution between Q1-4 in major prognostic variables such as tumour size, liver metastases and de novo disease status indicate a confounded relationship between AUC1 quartiles and OS. Diagnostics were provided for the updated OS CPH model that did not indicate any violation of the PH assumption for any of the variables TUMSBL (tumour size at baseline), DFI (disease free interval) or ALBBL (baseline albumin). Univariate KM-VPCs, stratified by ALBBL and TUMSBL quartiles showed concordance between observed and model-derived survival probability (data not shown).

The exposure-safety analyses are acceptable and conclusions supported. The exposure-safety models were based on data from BC clinical trial populations, where the vast majority (n=841/843) received the intended dose of 6mg/kg. Exposure-safety relationship were found between DatoDXd dose at AUC1 and Grade ≥3 TEAEs, TEAEs, dose reduction, oral mucositis/stomatitis, and ocular surface events in the BC pool. No exposure-safety relationship between Dato-DXd and/or DXd and adjudicated, drug-related ILD was established in any pool.

Immunogenicity was investigated based on data from the TB02 study. It is concluded that ADA prevalence was numerically similar across Study TB02 and the reference BC pools (range: 20.0% to 21.6%). In addition, the data indicate that the majority of ADA positive participants were classified as treatment-emergent-nAb positive (14.7%). The percentage of participants who were ADA positive at baseline ranged from 3.3% to 4.1% and the number of patients with treatmentboosted antibodies was similarly low in the TB02 study (n=4, 1.3%).

Patients with TE-ADA positive status showed similar PFS as patients with ADA negative status, while visual inspection of the KM curves for TE-ADA positives and ICC seemed to cross multiple times. Given the low number of patients in the TE-ADA positives (n=47) especially at median PFS/OS (n~20), efficacy outputs are overall non-informative and it is accepted that TE-ADA positive status shows similar efficacy as ADA negatives.

## 2.3.6. Conclusions on clinical pharmacology

The pharmacokinetics and immunogenicity of Dato-DXd in adult patients with TNBC have been sufficiently determined in the clinical study TB-02 and shown to be comparable to Dato-DXd in other approved BC indications. In conclusion, the obtained clinical pharmacology data is overall supportive for approval of the use of Dato-DXd in TNBC.

## 2.4. Clinical efficacy

## 2.4.1. Main study(ies)

TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) is a Phase III, randomized, open-label, 2-arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with investigator's Choice of Chemotherapy (ICC) in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

## Methods

Figure 19 Schematic of Study Design

<!-- image -->

- a Participants whose tumours are PD-L1-negative, or participants whose tumours are PD-L1-positive and have: 1) relapsed after prior PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy for early-stage breast cancer, 2) comorbidities precluding PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy, or 3) no regulatory access to pembrolizumab (participant's country does not have regulatory approval at the time of screening).
- b If no prior taxane, or prior taxane in the (neo)adjuvant setting and DFI &gt; 12 months, paclitaxel or nab-paclitaxel; if prior taxane and DFI ≤ 12 months: capecitabine, carboplatin, or eribulin. For participants who relapse after being treated for early-stage breast cancer, DFI will be measured as the time between completion of treatment with curative intent (either date of primary breast tumour surgery or date of last dose of systemic anti-cancer therapy [not including endocrine therapy], whichever occurred last) and the first documented local or distant disease recurrence (either by biopsy or imaging). Adjuvant radiation therapy is not considered treatment with curative intent for the purpose of calculating DFI.

## Study participants

The most important criteria are listed here. The complete list will be available in the published clinical study results.

## Key inclusion criteria:

1. Participant must be ≥ 18 years at the time of screening.
2. Histologically or cytologically documented locally recurrent inoperable TNBC, which cannot be treated with curative intent, or metastatic TNBC (ER with &lt; 1%, PR &lt; 1% and HER2 with 0 or 1+ intensity on IHC or 2+ intensity on IHC and negative by in situ hybridization)
3. No prior chemotherapy or other systemic anti-cancer therapy for metastatic or locally recurrent inoperable breast cancer.
4. Not a candidate for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy, defined as:
- Participants whose tumours are PD-L1-negative, or
- Participants whose tumours are PD-L1-positive and have: 1) relapsed after prior PD-1/PDL1 inhibitor therapy for early-stage breast cancer, 2) comorbidities precluding PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy, or 3) no regulatory access to pembrolizumab [participant's country does

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

not have regulatory approval at the time of screening]), additionally, participants eligible for atezolizumab in countries with regulatory approval of atezolizumab should be excluded.

5. At least 1 measurable lesion not previously irradiated (based on RECIST 1.1)
6. ECOG PS 0 or 1 with no deterioration over the previous 2 weeks prior to baseline or day of first dosing.
7. Eligible for one of the chemotherapy options listed as ICC (paclitaxel, nab-paclitaxel, capecitabine, carboplatin, or eribulin) based on DFI and prior taxane exposure, per investigator assessment.
4. Note 1: Participants with locally recurrent inoperable or relapsed metastatic TNBC are expected to have been treated with (neo)adjuvant anthracycline, unless anthracycline was contraindicated or not considered the best treatment option for the participant in the opinion of the treating physician.
5. Note 2: Participants presenting with de novo metastatic TNBC are eligible for the study, if anthracycline is contraindicated or not considered the best treatment option for the participant in the opinion of the treating physician.

Note 3: Participants with known germline BRCA pathogenic variants (based on testing performed as part of routine clinical practice) presenting with metastatic or locally recurrent inoperable TNBC are eligible for the study, if another therapy (such as poly adenosine diphosphate-ribose polymerase [PARP] inhibitor) is not considered the best treatment option for the participant in the opinion of the treating physician.

10. Participants with a history of previously treated neoplastic spinal cord compression or asymptomatic, stable brain metastases, who require no treatment with corticosteroids or anticonvulsants may be included in the study, if they are no longer symptomatic and have recovered from acute toxic effects of radiotherapy. A minimum of 2 weeks must have elapsed between the end of radiotherapy and Cycle 1 Day 1. A minimum of 3 days must have elapsed between the end of corticosteroid therapy for central nervous system metastatic disease and Cycle 1 Day 1.

11. Adequate organ and bone marrow function within 7 days before randomisation

## Key exclusion criteria:

7. Uncontrolled or significant cardiac disease including:

- Myocardial infarction or uncontrolled/unstable angina within 6 months prior to Cycle 1 Day 1
- Congestive heart failure (New York Heart Association Class II to IV), or
- Uncontrolled or significant cardiac arrhythmia, or
- Uncontrolled hypertension (resting systolic blood pressure &gt; 180 mmHg or diastolic blood pressure &gt; 110 mmHg).

## 15. Prior exposure to:

- Any treatment (including ADC) containing a chemotherapeutic agent targeting topoisomerase I - TROP2-targeted therapy
- Prior treatment with same ICC agent

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Chloroquine/hydroxychloroquine without an adequate treatment washout period of &gt; 14 days prior to randomisation.

Note: Participants are eligible for enrolment into this study if they able to receive treatment with another ICC agent not previously received. Participants who have DFI &gt; 12 months and have received prior taxane in the (neo)adjuvant setting can still receive paclitaxel or nab-paclitaxel.

## Treatments

The study intervention arms were:

- Arm 1: Dato-DXd (6 mg/kg IV on Day 1, Q3W)
- Arm 2: ICC:

If no prior taxane, or prior taxane in the (neo)adjuvant setting and DFI &gt; 12 months:

- Paclitaxel (80 mg/m2 IV on Day 1, Day 8 and Day 15, Q3W)
- Nab-paclitaxel (100 mg/m2 IV Day 1, Day 8 and Day 15, every 4 weeks [Q4W])

If prior taxane and DFI ≤ 12 months:

- Capecitabine (1000 or 1250 mg/m2 oral BID on Days 1 to 14, Q3W); choice between the 2 doses will be determined by standard institutional practice.
- Eribulin mesylate (1.4 mg/m2 IV on Day 1 and Day 8, Q3W).
- Carboplatin (AUC 6 [using Calvert formula] IV Day 1, Q3W)

## Objectives

## Primary:

- To demonstrate superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of PFS, by BICR
- To demonstrate superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of OS

## Main secondary:

- To demonstrate superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of PFS, by investigator assessment.
- To demonstrate superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of objective response rate (ORR)
- To demonstrate the superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of duration of response (DoR)
- To demonstrate the superiority of Dato-DXd relative to ICC by assessment of disease control rate (DCR)

Several secondary outcomes concerning patient reported outcomes (PROs), safety, PK and immunogenicity are not described here. Complete list can be found in the Clinical Study Report.

## Outcomes/endpoints

## Primary endpoints

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The dual primary endpoints of the study are:

- PFS by BICR according to RECIST 1.1, defined as the time from the date of randomisation until progression per RECIST 1.1 as assessed by BICR, or death due to any cause.

- OS in the ITT population, defined as the time from the date of randomisation until death due to any cause regardless of whether the participant withdraws from randomised therapy or receives another anti-cancer therapy (i.e. date of death or censoring - date of randomisation +1).

The study was to be considered positive if either the PFS or the OS analysis results were statistically significant.

## Secondary endpoints

Main secondary endpoints:

ORR is defined as the proportion of participants who have a confirmed CR or PR, as determined by BICR/investigator assessment, per RECIST 1.1.

DoR is defined as the time from the date of first documented confirmed response until date of documented progression per RECIST 1.1, as assessed by BICR/investigator assessment or death due to any cause.

PFS by investigator assessment is defined as the time from the date of randomisation until the date of PD per RECIST 1.1 by investigator assessment or death by any cause in the absence of progression.

DCR at 12 weeks is defined as the percentage of participants who have a CR or PR or who have SD, per RECIST 1.1, as assessed by BICR/investigator assessment and derived from the raw tumour data for at least 11 weeks after randomisation.

PROs: PRO questionnaires to assess secondary participant-reported pain, breast and arm symptoms, physical functioning and global health status (GHS)/quality of life (QoL) endpoints, assessed using selected items from the European Organisation for Research and Treatment of Cancer (EORTC) item library (IL), as well as participant-reported symptomatic AEs measured by selected items from the Patient-Reported Outcomes version of the Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (PRO-CTCAE)/EORTC IL reflecting overall influence of the benefits and toxicity of the treatment from the participant's perspective.

Safety and tolerability of Dato-DXd is assessed in terms of AEs (graded by CTCAE version 5.0) and in terms of ECOG PS, vital signs, body weight, physical examination, Clinical chemistry, haematology, and urinalysis assessments, and Ophthalmologic assessments.

## Sample size

Approximately 1100 participants were to be screened/enrolled to achieve approximately 600 randomised to study intervention.

The primary (and final) PFS analysis for superiority was planned to occur after approximately 422 BICR PFS events across the Dato-DXd and ICC treatment groups (70% maturity). The final OS analysis for superiority was planned to occur after approximately 340 OS events from the intent-totreat (ITT) population across the Dato-DXd and the ICC arms (57% maturity).

The study was sized for dual primary endpoints to assess the efficacy of Dato-DXd versus ICC in the ITT population in terms of PFS; as assessed by BICR and OS.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Assuming the true PFS treatment effect under the alternative hypothesis is the hazard ratio (HR) of 0.6 for Dato-DXd versus ICC and the median PFS in ICC was 5.6 months, 422 PFS events from the ITT population (70% maturity) were estimated to provide greater than 99% power to demonstrate statistical significance at the 2-sided alpha level of 1.0%. Assuming the true OS treatment effect under the alternative hypothesis was the HR of 0.72 and the median OS in ICC is 20.6 months, 340 OS events (57% maturity) were estimated to provide approximately 82.7% power to demonstrate statistical significance at the 2-sided alpha level of 4.0%. If the PFS by BICR dual primary analysis crosses the efficacy threshold, OS was to be tested at the 2-sided alpha level of 5.0% and the analysis would provide approximately 85% power to demonstrate statistical significance.

## Randomisation

A total of 1277 participants were screened. Of these, 644 were randomised in a 1:1 ratio to one of the 2 treatment arms; 323 in the Dato-DXd arm and 321 in the ICC arm.

Randomisation was stratified by the following prognostic factors:

- Geographic region (Region 1 [US, Canada, Europe] vs Region 2 [Rest of World])
- DFI history (de novo vs prior DFI ≤ 12 months vs prior DFI &gt; 12 months)
- PD-L1 status (positive [CPS ≥ 10] vs negative [CPS &lt; 10])

A recruitment cap of approximately 20% of the randomised participants was applied to participants with a DFI ≤ 12 months.

A recruitment cap of approximately 10% of the randomised participants was applied to participants with PD-L1-positive tumours who would otherwise have been eligible for pembrolizumab but did not have regulatory access to pembrolizumab (participant's country did not have regulatory approval at the time of screening).

## Blinding (masking)

The study was an open-label study for the personnel at study sites, however the trial was conducted as a sponsor-blind..

## Statistical methods

## Analysis set for efficacy

The full analysis set (FAS) will include all randomised participants. The FAS will be used for all efficacy analyses and treatment groups will be compared on the basis of randomised study treatment, regardless of the treatment actually received. Participants who were randomised but did not subsequently receive treatment are included in the FAS in the treatment group to which they were randomised. The analysis of data will be done using the FAS population that follows the principles of ITT. Of note, the safety analysis set will include participants who received at least one dose of study medication.

## PFS primary analysis

PFS is defined as the time from the date of randomisation until the date of objective disease progression, as defined by RECIST 1.1, or death (by any cause in the absence of progression) regardless of whether the participant withdraws from randomised therapy, receives another anti-

## OS

Overall survival (OS) is defined as the time from the date of randomisation until death due to any cause regardless of whether the participant withdraws from randomised therapy or receives another anti-cancer therapy (i.e. date of death or censoring - date of randomisation +1).

Any participant not known to have died at the time of analysis will be censored based on the last recorded date on which the participant was known to be alive.

Evaluation-time bias (Interval censoring analysis) Subgroup analysis |

Inverse probability censoring weighted analysis Subgroup analysis                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

The dual primary endpoint of PFS (by BICR) was stratified by DFI history and PD-L1 status, and the dual primary endpoint of OS was stratified by DFI history.

Of note, geographic region was not included as a stratification factor in the analysis although it remains a randomisation factor. For OS, only DFI was included in the stratified analysis. Selected stratification factors were removed from the stratified analysis in accordance with the prespecified SAP rules because of the small number of events within certain strata. This pooling was implemented to ensure that the resulting strata contained sufficient number of events to support robust and reliable analyses.

The HRs together with their 95% confidence intervals (CIs) and p values were presented (a HR less than 1 favoured the Dato-DXd arm). The HR was estimated from a stratified Cox Proportional Hazards model (with ties = Efron), and the CI calculated using a profile likelihood approach.

Ascertainment bias was assessed by analysing the site investigator data which is a secondary efficacy endpoint.

## Sensitivity for OS

Sensitivity Analysis 1- Attrition bias: A sensitivity analysis for OS examining the censoring patterns to rule out attrition bias with regard to the primary treatment comparisons will be achieved by a KM plot of time to censoring where the censoring indicator of OS is reversed.

Sensitivity Analysis 2 - Stratification according to eCRF

Sensitivity Analysis 3 - Inverse probability censoring weighted analysis. To better understand the impact of subsequent anti-cancer therapies on OS, a sensitivity analysis of OS using the inverse probability censoring weights (IPCW) method (Mosier 2023, Xue &amp; Lai 2023) will be performed accounting for the impact of subsequent anti-cancer of interest. The subsequent anticancer therapies of interest include:

- SACITUZUMAB GOVITECAN,
- SACITUZUMAB GOVITECAN HZIY,
- TRASTUZUMAB DERUXTECAN,
- TRASTUZUMAB DERUXTECAN NXKI,
- \"SACITUZUMAB TIRUMOTECAN\", \"SKB264\", \"SKB-264\", \"MK2870\", \"MK-2870\", or any variants thereof.

Standardized weights are estimated using the ratio of the probabilities estimated from two models, one containing only baseline covariates, the other containing baseline and time dependent covariates. The numerator of the weights is estimated using the baseline covariate model and the denominator of the weights is estimated using the baseline and time-dependent covariate model. Covariates were suggested by the clinical study team. Sponsor may add or remove one or more baseline or time-dependent covariates as appropriate. For example, if there are insufficient numbers of subjects receiving subsequent therapies of interest to fit a model with all the listed covariates, covariates may be removed. Or if inclusion of one of the covariates violates the positivity assumption, covariates may be dropped. The final model will be specified in the output.

The baseline covariates include:

- Age at randomisation
- Stratifications factors (geographic region, DFI history, and PD-L1 status)
- HER2 status at baseline
- Number of metastatic sites
- Brain metastases
- Liver metastases

The time-dependent covariates include:

- ECOG
- AE (G3 or greater)
- PFS by investigator

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Subgroups

Subgroup analyses will be conducted comparing PFS by BICR and OS between the treatments in the following subgroups of the FAS:

1. Stratification factors at randomisation:
- Geographic region (Region 1 [US, Canada, Europe] versus Region 2 [Rest of World])
- DFI history (de novo versus prior DFI &lt;= 12 months versus prior DFI &gt; 12 months)
- PD-L1 status (positive [CPS ≥ 10] versus negative [CPS &lt; 10])
2. Exploratory factors:
- Age at randomisation (&lt;65 versus ≥65 years of age)
- Race (Asian versus non-Asian).
- Brain metastases (Yes versus No)
- Liver metastases (Yes versus No)
- Pre-selected choice of chemotherapy (paclitaxel, nab-paclitaxel, capecitabine, carboplatin, eribulin mesylate/eribulin)
- HER2 (IHC 0 versus IHC 1+ and IHC 2+/FISH/ISH -ve)
- ECOG performance status at baseline (0 versus 1)
- Number of metastatic sites at baseline (&lt;3 versus ≥3)
- Prior anti-PD-(L)1 therapy for breast cancer (Yes versus No)

## Multiplicity

To preserve the overall type 1 error (familywise error rate), a multiple testing procedure (MTP) including the dual primary endpoints was implemented. Hypotheses were tested in the MTP using alpha (test mass) splitting and alpha recycling, where the test mass that becomes available to the OS analyses (recycled) if the PFS dual primary analysis null hypothesis is rejected. Implementation of this pre-defined ordered testing procedure, including recycling, will control type I error at 5.0% (2-sided), among all dual primary hypotheses.

The overall alpha for this study is 0.05, which is split with 0.01 to evaluate PFS and 0.04 to evaluate OS. If the PFS dual primary analysis crosses the efficacy threshold, the 1.0% type I error allocated to the PFS endpoint will be reallocated (Burman et al 2009) to the OS endpoint for a total type I error of 5.0%. The MTP for the dual primary endpoints is described in Figure 20.

## No interim analyses performed

This clinical study report (CSR) presents the final analysis (FA) of this study, which includes FA of both PFS and OS. No previous interim analysis has been conducted nor reported. The primary analysis of PFS (presented in this CSR) was planned to occur after approximately 422 BICR PFS events across the Dato-DXd and ICC treatment groups, and the FA of OS (presented in this CSR) was planned to occur after approximately 340 OS events from the ITT population across the DatoDXd and ICC treatment groups. The required approximate number of BICR PFS and OS events to trigger the primary PFS and final OS analyses, respectively, had occurred as of the 25 August 2025 data cut off (DCO).

## Planned interim analyses:

Two IAs for OS analysis are planned. The first OS IA will occur at the primary PFS analysis (approximately 57% of target OS events) and the second when approximately 80% of the target OS events have occurred. The Haybittle-Peto procedure (Haybittle 1971, Peto et al 1976) will be used to calculate the alpha spending at the first IA, resulting in a large critical value and small nominal p-value (2-sided nominal p-value 0.0001). For both the second IA and the final OS analyses, the Lan DeMets approach that approximates the O'Brien and Fleming spending function will be used (Lan and DeMets 1983).

- n = Number of participants per category.

## Recruitment

The study was conducted at 198 active sites in 23 countries.

First patient was screened 16 May 2022. Start date submitted to EudraCT is listed as 16 June 2022. Study completion according to National Clinical Trials was 3 December 2025. The final clinical data lock date was 19 September 2025 and the study was unblinded on 22 September 2025.

A total of 248 (38.5%) participants remained on study follow-up at the time of the DCO (25 August 2025), including 142 (44.0%) participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 106 (33.0%) participants in the ICC arm.

## Conduct of the study

## Protocol amendments:

| DOCUMENT HISTORY - Document     | DOCUMENT HISTORY - Date of Issue   |
|---------------------------------|------------------------------------|
| Amendment 3 (Version 4.0)       | 30 Jul 2024                        |
| Amendment 2 (Version 3.0)       | 11 Apr 2023                        |
| Amendment 1 (Version 2.0)       | 13 Jan 2022                        |
| Original Protocol (Version 1.0) | 29 Nov 2021                        |

From version 3 to 4 no important changes were made.

From version 2 to 3 the following important changes were made:

- Exclusion of participants eligible for atezolizumab in countries with regulatory approval of atezolizumab.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Increase of target screening number from 800 to 1100 participants
- Clarification of wording for PD-L1 testing and removal of the possibility for use of local PDL1 IHC 22C3 pharmDx assay results until the central laboratory was set up
- Changed end of study definition, text added to clarify FDA and EU requirements, and to specify the relevant statistical considerations

From version 1 to 2 the following important changes were made (before study start):

- Removal of hormone receptor low population. To ensure that definition of TNBC is in accordance with published guidelines.
- Removal of unknown PD-L1 status and clarification of which participants are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

b The FAS includes all randomised participants.

c The SAF consists of all participants who received at least one dose of study treatment.

d The PROAS includes all randomised participants who had their PRO data collected per protocol.

e The PK analysis set includes all participants randomly assigned to study intervention who took at least one dose of study intervention (Dato-DXd) for whom any post-dose PK data are available.

f The ADA evaluable set consists of participants in the SAF who received at least one dose of Dato-DXd and with a non-missing ADA Dato-DXd result at any time.

The same participant could have been excluded from an analysis set for more than one reason.

A HR &lt; 1 favours Dato-DXd to be associated with a longer PFS than ICC.

The overall primary analysis was performed using a stratified Cox Proportional Hazards model with stratification variables DFI history and PD-L1 status from IRT system.

Cox Proportional Hazards model containing a term for treatment (without stratification factors) was performed.

The subgroup analysis was performed using a Cox Proportional Hazards model with treatment as the only covariate. Subgroup variables are based on eCRF data, except that PD-L1 status is based on central laboratory 22C3 testing, and the pre-selected ICC are from the IRT system.

Size of circle is proportional to the number of events.

Grey band represents the 95% CI for the HR for the overall (all participants primary) analysis.

Progression includes deaths in the absence of RECIST progression. Progression events that do not occur within 2 assessments of the last evaluable assessment (or randomisation) are censored. RECIST version 1.1.

* There are less than 20 events across both treatment groups in a subgroup and thus HR (95% CI) for the treatment comparison is not displayed.

N = number of participants per treatment group

## TROP2 in TNBC, biomarker report

The TB02 study included an objective to explore whether TROP2 protein expression level may correlate with clinical response from Dato-DXd in TNBC. TROP2 expression was measured by the

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

exploratory IHC Ventana FLA EPR20043 assay in tumour samples obtained as close as possible to the time of diagnosis of metastatic or inoperable disease.

TROP2 cell membrane IHC expression was assessed by pathologists by membrane H-score and then assigned to one of 3 subgroups. Samples that were not evaluable (NE) or missing were included in a missing/NE category.

The following subgroups were assessed:

- Low H-score: 0 to 99 (&lt; 100)
- Medium H-score: 100 to 199
- High H-score: 200 to 300 (≥ 200)

- Missing or NE

A total of 644 participants were included in the BFAS (biomarker full analysis set), of which 516 (80.1%) participants had an evaluable result with numerical value available for the H-score and were included in the IHC-EAS (IHC biomarker evaluable analysis set - primary analysis set evaluable). For 128 (19.9%) participants, IHC data were missing or non-evaluable due to lack of sample, low tumour content, local country restrictions (China and Brazil), or withdrawal of consent.

Figure 29 Boxplot of TROP2 IHC H-score by Treatment Arm (IHC-EAS)

<!-- image -->

Nominal p-value derived from Wilcoxon Rank-Sum test.

## Summary of main study(ies)

The following tables summarise the efficacy results from the main studies supporting the present application. These summaries should be read in conjunction with the discussion on clinical efficacy as well as the benefit risk assessment (see later sections).

## Summary of efficacy for Study D926PC00001 (TROPION-Breast02)

Title: A Phase 3, Open-label, Randomised Study of datopotamab Deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) Versus Investigator's Choice of Chemotherapy in Patients who are not Candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 Inhibitor Therapy in First-line Locally Recurrent Inoperable or Metastatic Triple-negative Breast Cancer (TROPION-Breast02)

| Study identifier   | D926PC00001                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
|--------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|                    | This was a Phase 3, randomised, multicentre, open label, sponsor blinded study of Dato-DXd versus investigator's choice of chemotherapy in participants who are not candidates for programmed death receptor-1 (PD-1)/programmed death-ligand 1 (PD-L1) inhibitor therapy in first-line locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TROPION-Breast02) |

## Notes

- a Calculated using the Kaplan-Meier technique.
- b Response required confirmation.
- c The HR and CI were estimated from a stratified Cox Proportional Hazards model with the Efron method to control for ties, the stratification factors of DFI history and PD-L1 status, and the CI calculated using a profile likelihood approach.
- d An HR &lt; 1 favoured Dato DXd, for example to be associated with a longer OS or PFS than ICC.
- e P-values were generated using the stratified log-rank test adjusting for stratification variables of DFI history and PD-L1 status for PFS and DFI history for OS.
- f The analysis was performed using logistic regression adjusting for factors of treatment, DFI history, and PD-L1 status with the CI calculated using a profile likelihood approach and the p-value calculated using a stratified log-rank test adjusting for stratification factors of geographic region, DFI history, and PD-L1 status.
- g An odds ratio &gt; 1 favoured Dato-DXd compared to ICC.

## Supportive study(ies)

## Design:

TP01 is a Phase I, 2-part, multicentre, multiple-dose, FIH study in participants with advanced solid tumours. The main objective of part 1 was to investigate the safety and tolerability and to determine the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) and the recommended dose for expansion (RDE) of Dato-DXd. The main objective of part 2 was to investigate the safety and tolerability of Dato-DXd.

Efficacy endpoints included ORR, DCR, CBR, DoR, TTR, PFS, and OS. The efficacy endpoints were evaluated by BICR and the investigator. Efficacy analyses of TP01 used the FAS, which included all participants who received at least one dose of Dato-DXd.
