## 2.4.2. Discussion on clinical efficacy

## Design and conduct of clinical studies

The efficacy assessment of the applied extension of indication is based on the pivotal trial Tropion02 (TB02) trial. The study was designed as a phase III, randomized, open-label, 2-arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with investigator's Choice of Chemotherapy (ICC) in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC in the first-line setting, who were not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

The PD-L1 criteria consisted of several parts. Patients with PD-L1 negative tumours or patients whose tumours were positive but had relapsed after prior PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor treatment in the early breast cancer (EBC) setting, comorbidities precluding PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor treatment or no regulatory access to pembrolizumab. This represents a source of heterogeneity as these patients are not prognostically equal. However, this was alleviated to some degree by including PD-L1 status (CPS≥10 vs CPS &lt; 10) as a stratification factor.

The choice of comparator chemotherapy (ICC) was pre-defined prior to randomisation and consisted of the current SOC monotherapy chemotherapy options such as taxanes, eribulin and capecitabine with the exception of anthracyclines (as well as docetaxel and combination chemotherapy which are less used options in this context). During CHMP SA, inclusion either of anthracyclines as an option or of a criterion specifying prior use at inclusion was advised. Despite this, and the fact that this option is clinically relevant and included in the ESMO treatment guideline on TNBC, anthracyclines were not included as a treatment option in the ICC. However, it is acknowledged that there is no clear superiority of one monotherapy agent over others, when considering capecitabine, taxanes and anthracyclines, and there is no ideal sequence of treatments that can be applied to all patients. The TB02 trial recruited 1/3 of patients with de novo disease status, which due to the omission of anthracycline monotherapy could only receive part of the ESMO endorsed first-line treatment. The inclusion of \"not considered the best treatment option in the opinion of the treating physician\" is not successfully justified as 9-15% of study population received anthracyclines as a subsequent therapy. While this design choice limits external generalisability of the TB02 trial results, it does not preclude efficacy demonstration in this procedural setting, and it is considered acceptable given that the TROPION-02 trial is in line with the recent clinical trial context (i.e. IMpassion130 and IMpassion131 and KEYNOTE-355 studies). Additional information has been included in the SmPC section 5.1 specifying the omission of anthracyclines in the ICC.

The inclusion criteria of TB02 reflect the targeted population.

Several changes to the study protocol and procedures were made after study initiation from protocol version 2.0 to 3.0. These were mostly clarifications that did not change definitions of primary or secondary outcomes significantly. Regarding changes to PD-L1 testing, the Applicant has clarified that all participants were enrolled after the availability of central PD-L1 testing. The number of patients who discontinued from study treatment for \"other reasons\" were more than double for the ICC arm than for the Dato-DXd (21 (6.8%) patients compared to 10 (3.1%) patients). Reasons for early discontinuation were not recorded systematically. Demographic variables for patients with early study discontinuation were reasonably similar to each other prognostically and impact on study efficacy outcomes is considered limited.

The study screened 1277 patients in order to include 644 patients. With a patient representativeness of 644/1277 (~50%), the generalizability to the population of TNBC cancer patients may be limited, however this does not affect the internal validity and hypotheses of the

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

study. Also, a large extent of screen failures were due to the fact that many patients had not been PD-L1 tested at the initial diagnosis (44% (262/595 screen failures)).

The statistical methodology specified the full analysis set (FAS) as all randomised participants. Participants were analysed on the basis of randomised study treatment, regardless of the treatment actually received. This is aligned with the ITT principle and endorsed. Superiority of PFS (by BIRC) and OS were tested by stratified log-rank test. PFS was stratified by DFI history and PDL1 status, and the dual primary endpoint of OS was stratified by DFI history. Selected stratification factors were removed from the stratified analysis in accordance with the pre-specified SAP rules because of the small number of events within certain strata. This pooling was implemented to ensure that the resulting strata contained sufficient number of events to support robust and reliable analyses. The HR was estimated from a stratified (same as above) Cox Proportional Hazards model, and the CI calculated using a profile likelihood approach, which presumably has better coverage than the Wald CI. This is endorsed.

The primary analysis of PFS specified the following intercurrent events: withdrawal from randomized therapy, receiving another anti-cancer therapy or clinically progressing prior to RECIST 1.1 progression. These ICE's are addressed by \"treatment policy strategy\". However, if the participant progresses or dies immediately after 2 or more consecutive missed visits, the participant will be censored. A number of sensitivity analyses were performed. Of particular interest were: no censoring to events regardless of two missed visits, initiation of new anti-cancer therapy deemed event and evaluation time bias all consistent with the primary analysis.

The dual primary endpoints, PFS and OS, were tested with a hierarchical structure allowing the initial 1% allocated to PFS to be recycled to OS, if PFS was significant. The multiplicity strategy (Burman et al 2009) is endorsed. This application is based on 408 PFS (BICR) events and 349 OS events, close to the original planned final analyses, i.e., 422 and 340 events. The final analyses of both PFS and OS were performed based on DCO 25 August 2025 (). No previous interim analysis was conducted.

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

Baseline characteristics were well-balanced between arms and the minor differences observed are not expected to impact validity of the study results. The majority of patients were included in Rest of World n=404/644 (62.7%), with a large majority of patients in Region 1 (US/Canada/Europe) recruited in Europe (n=187/240)

Disease characteristics were also well-balanced in the ITT, no marked differences in distribution of major prognostics factors were observed between the Dato-DXd arm and the ICC, such as diseasefree interval (DFI) below 1 year (20.7% vs 20.6%) and median time from TNBC diagnosis of 1.09 and 1.08 years, respectively. There were more patients discontinuing treatment in the ICC arm than in the Dato-DXd arm (97.4% compared to 84.9%) and at DCO 14.1% compared to 2.6% were still ongoing allocated treatment with Dato-DXd or ICC, respectively.

Results from the final analysis at DCO showed a total of 408 out of 644 participants (63.4% maturity) recorded to have had PFS events based on BICR, including 199 events out of 323 randomised participants (61.6%) in the Dato-DXd arm and 209 events out of 321 randomised participants (65.1%) in the ICC arm. The median PFS by BICR was 10.84 months (95% CI: 8.57, 12.98) in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 5.55 months (95% CI: 4.96, 6.97) in the ICC arm, representing a clinically relevant difference of 5.29 months in favour of Dato-DXd with a HR of 0.57 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.69). Sensitivity analyses of PFS were consistent with the main analysis with HRs ranging from 0.48 - 0.60. Censoring was balanced, with 124 (38.4%) censored in the Dato-DXd

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

arm and 112 (34.9%) in the ICC with a slightly higher percentage of patients being censored at day 1 in the ICC compared to the Dato-DXd arm (14 (4.4%) patients compared to 5 (1.5%) patients in the Dato-DXd arm).

At DCO, OS data had reached 54.2% maturity with 349 events observed out of a total of 644 randomised participants. The median OS was 23.66 months (95% CI: 19.81, 25.63) in the Dato-DXd arm vs 18.66 months (95% CI: 15.97, 21.78) in the ICC arm with a HR of 0.79 (95% CI: 0.64, 0.98). Overall censoring was acceptable with 155 (48.0%) censored patients in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 140 (43.6%) in the ICC. No sensitivity analyses of OS are specified in the protocol or amendments, but appear in the original SAP 1.0 dated 10 August 2022, which changed markedly with the SAP 2.0 dated 7 February 2025 (~6 months before FA DCO). Here analyses were added to assess the impact of baseline covariates, time-dependant variables and subsequent anti-cancer therapies on OS via an IPCW model. Results showed marked improvement of OS HRs from 0.79 (95% CI: 0.64, 0.98) in the original analysis to 0.65 (95% CI: 0.51, 0.84). This difference might be driven by the impact of subsequent therapy rather than baseline factors and is expanded upon below.

Subgroup analyses of PFS and OS were pre-specified in the protocol SAP version 4.0. PFS subgroup analyses are consistent with the main result with HR CIs generally below the 1.00 threshold in sufficiently large subgroups. Most notably, a sufficiently large subgroup analyses of geographic region with 79 and 70 events in each arm shows that the benefit of Dato-DXd compared to ICC in patients from US, Canada and Europe is lower than what was observed for Rest of World (HR 0.69 (95% CI: 0.50, 0.95) compared to HR 0.50 (95% CI: 0.39, 0.64)). While this difference is acceptable in itself, it suggests that relevant regional differences could be present in the dataset.

Subgroup analyses of OS in patients treated with Dato-DXd compared to ICC in region 1 i.e. US, Canada, Europe showed an HR of 1.22 (95% CI: 0.86, 1.73) compared to an HR of 0.60 (95% CI: 0.46, 0.79) in Rest of World. This analysis was performed post-hoc for the European population only (n=187, 78% of region 1 population) and resulted in an HR of 1.34 (95% CI: 0.90, 1.99). Sensitivity analyses were provided looking into baseline characteristics and subsequent ADC use.

Regarding baseline characteristics, a post-hoc OS IPCW sensitivity analyses showed that OS HR improved when accounting for prognostic variables at baseline (HR 0.90 (95% CI: 0.54, 1.51)). Importantly, the proportion of patients with liver metastases in the Dato-DXd arm of region 1 was 2.5% higher than in the ICC arm (30.8% vs 28.3%). This is a small difference, but compared to Region 2, the between arm (Dato-arm vs ICC arm) difference was -4.2% (27.6% vs 31.8%), which highlights that, not only was the proportional difference of patients with liver metastases numerically higher in the ICC arm in Region 2 compared to the ICC arm of Region 1, the difference in itself also favoured the ICC in Region 1 whereas in Region 2, this baseline prognostic factor numerically favoured Dato-DXd arm (4.2% less patients with liver metastases compared with the ICC). A similar shift in favourable/unfavourable position of baseline prognostic factors were seen for ECOG PS 0, Brain metastases and number of metastatic sites (3 and &gt;3). Furthermore, in the European population only, the between baseline prognostic factors such as liver metastases and number of metastatic sites &gt;3 were even more unbalanced in favour of the ICC arm (5.6% and 8.8% arm differences respectively). In an additional requested multivariate Cox proportional hazards model OS sensitivity analysis adjusting for imbalanced prognostic characteristics, it is noted that the adjusted OS HR in Region 1 was 1.02 (95% CI: 0.71, 1.48) from an unadjusted OS HR of 1.22 (0.86, 1.73) and the adjusted OS HR in European population was 1.11 (0.73, 1.69) compared to 1.34 (0.90, 1.99) in the unadjusted analysis.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The potential differential OS results in Region 1 and the European population could also arise because of an unbalance of subsequent therapies received between arms. Indeed, a significantly higher proportion of ICC patients received subsequent ADC use compared to the Dato-DXd arm (45.7% vs 20.4% for sacituzumab govitecan and 13.8% vs 3.2% trastuzumab deruxtecan). In Region 1, 45.0% in the ICC arm received a subsequent ADC, while this was only 20.0% in the Dato-DXd arm. This was markedly different from Region 2, where 20.9% in the ICC arm received a subsequent ADC, while 11.8% did so in the Dato-DXd arm. A two-stage OS sensitivity analysis adjusting for ADC use only, demonstrated OS HRs of 0.83 (95% CI: 0.59, 1.18) and 0.79 (0.53, 1.18) for Region 1 and the European population, respectively.

Although there are limitations on the interpretation of these adjusted analyses, the results of the additional analyses reasonably support the claim that the above-mentioned descriptive imbalances in poor prognostic baseline variables likely partly account for the differential OS result in Region 1 and the European population and the adjusted OS HRs are not considered detrimental. In addition, the variability seen in the OS measures between regions suggest that the extent of the overall impact of Dato-DXd on overall survival in breast cancer may depend on the other treatments available and the evolving treatment paradigm.

Almost half of the OS events are unaccounted for in TB02; however, OS updates are not possible as the Applicant did not collect OS data beyond the DCO of the primary outcome. Given the maturity of the currently available OS data (54.2% maturity), the trajectory is expected to remain stable even with more maturity and no further updates are required.

Secondary outcomes were all consistent with the primary outcomes showing clinically relevant benefit of Dato-DXd on PFS by investigator, ORR, BOR, DOR, TFST and PFS2 compared to ICC. Median PFS by investigator was 9.63 months (95% CI: 7.36, 11.20) in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 5.22 months (95% CI: 4.21, 5.59) in the ICC arm with a corresponding HR of 0.56 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.67), which is somewhat lower than PFS by BICR where median PFS was 10.84 months in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 5.55 months in the ICC. However, this analysis was also based on more events than the BICR analysis with 74.6% and 80.4% total events compared 61.6% and 65.1% in the BICR analysis for Dato-DXd and ICC arms, respectively. This is considered acceptable as between arm discordance rate between analyses is 13% for the Dato-DXd arm and 15.3% for the ICC arm. Confirmed ORR was 62.5% for the Dato-DXd arm and 29.3% for the ICC with more CR in the Dato-DXd arm than in the ICC (9.0% compared to 2.5%). Subgroup analyses of ORR odds ratios showed consistent response across all subgroups.

Patient-reported outcomes suffered from a high rate of missing-at-baseline (32.4% in the DatoDXd arm and 36.4% in the ICC). Overall, a positive development in GHS/QoL favouring the DatoDXd arm was indicated by time to first deterioration (TTD) on GHS/QoL (HR 0.64; 95% CI: 0.46, 0.88) with a median TTD of 23.49 months Dato-DXd compared to 8.28 months in the ICC. Focusing on specific subscales of the EORTC-IL146 (comparable to the QLQ-C30), Dato-DXd seems to postpone TTD in diarrhoea and fatigue, but not in nausea/vomiting, appetite loss and constipation, which is somewhat contradicting the major benefit seen on GHS/QoL. The open-label design and lack of multiplicity control significantly hamper the interpretability of PRO data, which should not be reflected in the SmPC.

Exploratory endpoints displaying PFS and OS by TROP2 levels show that efficacy is most likely preserved across different levels of TROP2, although a clear beneficial effect on OS results for the medium and low groups is less convincing. Of note, in the low TROP-2 expression group, OS HR was 0.94 (95% CI 0.58-1.50) with nearly overlapping OS KM curves and for PFS the benefit was only marginal (HR 0.78, 95% CI 0.49-1.24). This is compared to TROP2 high, where OS HR was 0.68 (95% CI 0.49-0.93) and PFS HR was 0.49 (0.36-0.66). However, the at-risk population was

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

markedly lower for TROP2 low analyses than for TROP2 high (102 compared to 311, respectively) resulting in very wide confidence intervals. With this uncertainty, it is considered difficult to conclude upon the impact of TROP2 expression and this is not pursued further.

## 2.4.3. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

The pivotal study TB02 demonstrated a clinically relevant and superior efficacy of datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) compared to standard of care (single-agent chemotherapy) regarding PFS in the ITT. The results in terms of OS were also statistically significant.

## 2.5. Clinical safety

## Introduction

The safety profile of datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) was previously assessed in the TROPION-Breast01 Study, the pivotal study which led to the approval of Dato-DXd in patients with advanced and metastatic breast cancer as later line therapy. In this study, the safety profile of Dato-DXd was found to be characterized by gastrointestinal toxicity, stomatitis, ILD, eye disorders, and to a lesser degree haematological toxicity and neurotoxicity. Adverse events of special interest are known to be ILD/pneumonitis, infusion-related reactions, oral mucositis/stomatitis, mucosal inflammation other than oral mucositis/stomatitis and ocular surface events.

## Safety data pooling strategy

The safety assessment of this application is based on the ongoing pivotal Study TROPION-Breast02 (TB02). Supportive safety data are provided from Studies TROPION-Breast01 (TB01), TROPIONPanTumor02 (TP02), TROPION-PanTumor01 (TP01).

For the purpose of this submission, safety data from pivotal Study TB02 and the breast cancer population from the supportive Studies TB01, TP02 and TP01 were pooled.

Table 43 Safety Data Pooling Strategy: Clinical Studies and Number of Participants Included in Datasets

|------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|

a See Table 2 for an overview of studies included in the pooled analyses.

b Total exposure of Dato-DXd (months) is calculated as (min (last date where dose &gt; 0 + 20, date of death, date of DCO) – first dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12).

Total exposure of Paclitaxel (months) is calculated as (min (last paclitaxel dose date where dose &gt; 0 + 6, date of death, date of DCO) – first Paclitaxel dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12).

Total exposure of Nab-paclitaxel (months) is calculated as (min (last Nab-paclitaxel dose date where dose &gt; 0 + W, date of death, date of DCO) – first Nab-paclitaxel dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12), where W = 6 if the last Nab-paclitaxel dose was scheduled on Day 1 or Day 8, and W = 13 if the last Nab-paclitaxel dose was scheduled on Day 15.

Total exposure of Capecitabine (months) is calculated as (min (last Capecitabine dose date where dose &gt; 0 + W, date of death, date of DCO) – first Capecitabine dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12), where W = 0 if the last Ca     = e   o      s e  se was scheduled on Day 14.

Total exposure of Carboplatin (months) is calculated as (min (last Carboplatin dose date where dose &gt; 0 + 20, date of death, date of DCO) – first Carboplatin dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12).

Total exposure of Éribulin mesylate/Eribulin(months) is calculated as (min (last Eribulinmesylate/Eribulin dose date where dose &gt; 0 + W, date of death, date of DCO) – first Eribulin mesylate/Eribulin dose date + 1) / (365.25 / 12), where W = 6 if the last Eribulin mesylate/Eribulin dose was scheduled on Day 1 and W = 13 if the last Eribulin mesylate/Eribulindose was scheduled on Day 8.

## Adverse events

AEs presented in the safety evaluation are treatment emergent. The following AEs were considered treatment emergent:

- AEs with an onset date on or after the first dose of study intervention and within 28 (+ 7) days after the last dose of study intervention or up to the day prior to the start of subsequent therapy, whichever occurred first.
- Worsening of pre-existing events on or after the first dose of study intervention and within 28 (+ 7) days after the last dose of study intervention or up to the day prior to the start of subsequent therapy, whichever occurred first.

Per protocol, all ILD/pneumonitis events regardless of severity were to be reported beyond the 28 (+ 7)-day safety follow-up period.

Radiotherapy is not considered as a subsequent anti-cancer therapy in TB02, TB01, and TP02.

At each level of summarisation, a participant is counted once if the participant reported one or more AEs.

## Adverse Drug Reactions (ADR) in the SmPC

Following the FA of Study TB02, new ADRs for Dato-DXd were identified, including neutropenia (PTs: neutropenia, neutrophil count decreased), leukopenia (leukopenia, white blood cell count decreased), keratopathy (PTs: corneal epithelium defect, corneal lesion, injury corneal, keratopathy), increased amylase (amylase increased, hyperamylasaemia), and rhinorrhoea that have been added in the table of adverse reaction in SmPC section 4.8.

A summary of the frequency and severity of ADRs for Dato DXd based on the BC 6 mg/kg pool is provided below. ILD and keratitis are the 2 important identified risks for Dato DXd; ILD occurred in 2.82% participants in TB02 Dato-DXd arm and 2.97% participants in the BC 6 mg/kg pool, and keratitis occurred in 17.87% participants in the TB02 Dato-DXd arm and 16.65% participants in the BC 6 mg/kg pool.

Percentages are based on the number of participants in the Safety Analysis Set. TEAEs were coded using MedDRA Version 28.0 and graded using NCI CTCAE version 5.0 for TB02, TB01, and TP02, and NCI CTCAE version 4.03 before protocol amendment version 5.0 and NCI CTCAE version 5.0 afterwards for TP01.

A TEAE is defined as an adverse event with a start or worsening date on or after the start of study treatment until 35 days since the last dose of study treatment (TP01), or earliest date between 35 days since the last dose of treatment and the start date of the first subsequent therapy (TB02, TB01, and TP02). Radiotherapy is not considered as a subsequent anti-cancer therapy.

## Immunological events

There was no apparent effect of ADA on safety of Dato-DXd. See section 2.3 Immunogenicity.

## Safety related to drug-drug interactions and other interactions

No new data regarding safety related to drug-drug interactions have been presented in this application.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Post marketing experience

Since the first approval of Dato-Dxd on 04 April 2025 through 26 June 2025 (DCO for the latest periodic safety update report), the estimated cumulative patient exposure was 214.5 patient-years. No new safety concerns have been identified based on post-marketing reports.

## 2.5.1. Discussion on clinical safety

In study TB02, the safety profile of datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) was compared to investigator's choice of chemotherapy (or ICC, being taxanes, capecitabine, eribulin or carboplatin) in the first line setting of unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer. Data for the evaluation of the safety of Dato-DXd treatment in the target population (HR/PR-negative/HER2negative breast cancer) are derived from N = 319 from the pivotal phase III trial TROPION￼Breast02 (TB02) with supportive data contributed by the TROPION￼Breast01 (TB01), TROPION-PanTumor02 (TP02), TROPION￼PanTumor01 (TP01), TROPION￼Lung01 (TL01), and TROPION￼Lung05 (TL05) studies. The pooled data from these supportive studies and the pivotal TB02 comprise data from 1365 patients, of which 841 are patients with breast cancer (BC). The magnitude of the datasets included for assessment of the safety profile of Dato-Dxd are considered acceptable.

## Exposure

At the time of the Final Analysis DCO (25 August 2025), 319 participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 309 in the investigator's choice of chemotherapy (ICC) arm had received study treatment in Study TB02. Median duration of exposure was 8.51 months in the Dato-DXd arm and 4.14 months in the ICC arm, respectively. The median relative dose intensity (RDI) was 98.64% in the Dato-DXd arm and 95.42% in the ICC arm. In the TB02 Study 60.5% had a treatment duration &gt; 6 months and the median number of cycles initiated was 12.

Drug interruptions were reported for 13 (4.1%) participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 7 (2.3%) participants in the ICC arm. The primary reason for drug interruption in the Dato-DXd and ICC arms was AEs (9 [2.8%] and 4 [1.3%], respectively).

Similar proportions of participants in the TB02 Dato-DXd arm and the BC 6 mg/kg pool had drug interruption (4.1% and 2.6%, respectively) or dose reduction (28.8% and 26.5%). Cycle delay was required for a similar proportion of participants in the TB02 Dato-DXd arm compared with the BC 6 mg/kg pool (78.7% versus 75.9%).

The extent of exposure time is considered acceptable for safety evaluation.

## Summary of Adverse Events

AEs were reported for 98.7% of participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 94.8% of participants in the ICC arm.

AEs of CTCAE Grade ≥ 3 were reported for 43.3% of participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 37.5% of participants in the ICC arm. SAEs occurred in 16.9% of participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 15.5% of participants in the ICC arm. Fatal AEs during study treatment or 28-day follow-up were reported for 1 (0.3%) participant in the Dato-DXd arm and 5 (1.6%) participants in the ICC arm.

AEs leading to discontinuation of study intervention were reported for 4.7% of participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 7.8% of participants in the ICC arm. The proportion of patients with AEs leading to dose interruption was similar in the treatment arms (35.4% of participants in the Dato-DXd and 31.7% of participants in the ICC arm), while the proportion of patients with AEs leading to dose

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

reduction was higher in the Dato-Dxd arm (28.2% versus 18.4%).

## Adverse Events

At the FA DCO (25 August 2025) similar proportions of patients experienced any TEAE in the DatoDxd (98.7%) and ICC arm (94.8%). The system organ classes with the most frequently reported TEAEs in the Dato-Dxd arm were gastrointestinal disorders (83.7% versus 55.0% in the ICC arm), skin and subcutaneous disorders (62.1% versus 51.1%), eye disorders (54.2% versus 13.3%), respiratory, thoracic and mediastinal disorders (37.0% versus 27.5%).

The most commonly reported TEAEs by Preferred Term for Dato-DXd were stomatitis (58.3% versus 9.7% in the ICC arm), nausea (48.3% versus 22.3%), alopecia (42.9% versus 34.6%), constipation (39.8% versus 16.8%) and dry eye (26.3% versus 4.9%), which were considerably more frequent in the Dato-Dxd arm than in the ICC arm. All were reported in a higher proportion of patients in the Dato-DXd arm than in the ICC arm (≥10% difference, except for alopecia). Other frequently reported TEAEs for Dato-Dxd were vomiting (22.6%), anaemia (20.7%), decreased appetite (19.1%), cough (18.5%), fatigue (17.6%), asthenia (15.4%), keratitis (13.8%) and rash (12.9%). TEAEs reported in at least 5% of subjects in either treatment arm at ≥2-fold higher incidences in the Dato-DXd arm included keratitis, skin hyperpigmentation, conjunctivitis, lacrimation increased, pyrexia, dry mouth and oropharyngeal pain. Overall the types and reported frequencies of TEAEs were comparable to those of the BC Dato-Dxd pooled studies.

Conversely, the most frequently reported TEAEs in the ICC arm included nausea and alopecia (but at a lower frequency than with Dato-DXd), anaemia, neutropenia, neutrophil count decrease, diarrhoea, fatigue (similar to the frequency with Dato-DXd), and white blood cell count decrease. Overall haematological toxicity was higher in the ICC arm. The TEAEs reported were in line with the known safety profiles of the chemotherapy options in the control arm.

## Dose modifications

The proportion of patients with any TEAE leading to a dose reduction was highest in the Dato-Dxd arm with 90 (28.2%) versus 57 (18.4%); however, when adjusted for exposure, the rates of AEs leading to dose reduction were 31.62 per 100 patient-years in the Dato-DXd arm and 39.02 per 100 patient-years in the ICC arm. The most common AE leading to dose reduction in the Dato-Dxd arm was stomatitis (11.3%), followed by amylase increase (2.8%), keratitis (2.5%9 and asthenia (1.9%). The most common AE leading to dose reduction in the ICC arm was peripheral neuropathy and neutropenia. Dose interruptions occurred slightly more often in the Dato-Dxd arm (35.4% versus 31.7%), the main causes being amylase increase and stomatitis. Overall, dose modifications (either reduction or interruption) were more frequent in the Dato-Dxd arm (in 48.0% versus 41.1%), with the dominating AEs being stomatitis, amylase increase, keratitis in the Dato-Dxd arm and neuropathy and neutropenia in the ICC arm. The MAH took the opportunity to clarify in section 4.2 of the SmPC that treatment with Dato-Dxd should discontinue in case there is a requirement for further dose reduction beyond the second one.

## Serious Adverse Events

Overall the frequency of TEAEs with CTCAE grade 2 or 3 was slightly higher in the Dato-Dxd arm (and aligned with the frequencies in the BC Dato-Dxd Pool) than in the ICC arm, (47.6% vs. 43.4% and 39.5% vs. 30.1%, respectively) whereas grade 4 and 5 events were similar. The most common AEs of CTCAE Grade ≥ 3 were amylase increase (9.7%) and stomatitis (8.5%) in the Dato-Dxd arm and neutropenia + neutrophil count decrease + white blood cell count decrease (overall 17.8%) and anaemia (5.2%) in the ICC arm.

## Adverse Events of Special Interest (AESI)

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## ILD/pneumonitis

Interstitial lung disease (ILD), including pneumonitis, is an identified AE of special interest for Dato-DXd and fatal cases have been observed. Clinically, symptomatic ILD and pneumonitis are indistinguishable conditions and their treatment is identical (systemic corticosteroids). Therefore, ILD/pneumonitis should be grouped together when reporting their incidence. In TB02 the incidence of all-causality ILD/pneumonitis as reported by investigator was 16 (5.0%) in the Dato-DXd versus 4 (1.3%) in the ICC arm. In the TB02, all ILD/pneumonitis events assessed by investigator, were subsequently subject to adjudication by an independent ILD adjudication committee, which is an acceptable practice (EMEA/CHMP/EWP/5872/03) to verify the presence or not of ILD or pneumonitis.

Discontinuation due to ILD/pneumonitis occurred in 5 patients (1.5%). One CTCAE grade 5 event of ILD/pneumonitis was reported in the TB02 trial. The risk of ILD/pneumonitis is appropriately reflected in the SmPC and the RMP.

## Infusion-related reactions

As per FA, IRRs occurred in 8.8% of subjects in the Dato-DXd arm versus 9.4% in the ICC arm. Most events were grade 1 or 2, and only 1 grade 3 event was reported. Only a few patients required dose interruption (1.3%) and one patient discontinued study treatment.

## Oral mucositis/ stomatitis

A significantly higher proportion of patients in the Dato-DXd arm had an AESI of oral mucositis/stomatitis in the Dato-DXd arm compared with in the ICC arm (63.0 % versus 12.9%, respectively). The most commonly reported preferred term contributing to oral mucositis/stomatitis AESIs in the Dato-DXd arm was stomatitis (58.3%). Most events were grade 1 or 2, while grade 3 events occurred in 8.5% of subjects in the Dato-DXd arm. Events were predominantly managed by dose reduction (11.3%). Few patients had a dose interruption (3.8%) and stomatitis did not lead to drug discontinuation. Preventive measures and supportive care options are appropriately described in the SmPC section 4.4.

## Ocular surface events

Ocular surface events is a known risk of antibody-drug conjugates. In the TB02 trial ocular surface events (including keratitis) was reported in 51.1% of subjects in the Dato-DXd arm and in 9.4% the ICC arm. In the Dato-DXd arm most events were CTCAE grade 1 (56.0%) and grade 2 (17.6%), a minority were grade 3 (6.6%) and there were 3 (0.9%) grade 4 events. The most common PTs in the AESI of ocular surface events in the Dato-DXd arm were dry eye (26.3%), keratitis (13.8%%), conjunctivitis (7.8%), lacrimation increased (6.6%) and punctuate keratitis (4.1%). At DCO 41.1% of the events were resolved, 9.8% were resolving and 49.1% were not resolved. The median time to resolution for grade 2 ocular events was 86 days and for grade 3 the median time was 66.5 days. Among the resolved events, no sequelae were reported. Events resulted in dose interruption (n=20, 6.3%), or dose reduction (n=15, 4.7%), and three subjects (0.9%) discontinued from study treatment. At DCO, 58.9% of patients with ocular surface events (i.e. 96 of 149 patients with ocular surface events considered treatment related in the Dato-DXd arm) had unresolved ocular surface events. Since no follow-up data were collected after the final DCO, the median duration of ocular surface events cannot be established, nor whether a proportion of ocular surface events remains unresolved. Overall, the frequency and severity degree of ocular surface events is similar to what has already been described for Dato-DXd. Keratopathy was identified as a new ADR and has been added in the table of adverse reactions in SmPC section 4.8.

## Deaths

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The investigator reported the primary cause of death as related to the disease under investigation in the majority of cases in both treatment arms (Dato-DXd: 142 [44.4%]; ICC: 149 [46.4%] participants), with 'Other' primary causes of death in 25 (7.7%) participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 26 (8.1%) participants in the ICC arm (Table 55). AEs were reported as the primary cause of death for 1 (0.3%) participant in the Dato-DXd arm (CTCAE Grade 5 ILD/pneumonitis) and 6 (1.9%) participants in the ICC arm. The narratives presented for the category 'other' do not suggest the presence of any treatment-related deaths in this category. Further, no imbalance in the number of patients with 'other' causes of death is observed between the two treatment arms.

## Laboratory findings

## Haematology

Most subjects had normal or CTCAE grade 1 for haematology tests post-baseline. Grade 3 or 4 was reported in a limited number of subjects (&lt; 5%). The majority of shifts from baseline were 1-grade or 2-grade. Anaemia was reported as TEAE in 20.7% and as an SAE in 1.3%. Dose interruption was seen in 1.3% due to neutropenia, in 1.9% due to neutrophil count decrease and in 0.6% due to white blood cell count decrease. There were no cases of febrile neutropenia in the Dato-DXd arm. Overall haematological toxicity was limited and manageable in the Dato-DXd arm.

## Liver function

At least 1 elevated liver enzyme test was reported for 52.1% of participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 52.8% of participants in the ICC arm. Increases ≥ 3 x ULN in AST or ALT were reported in 8.2% in the Dato-DXd arm and 11.0% in the ICC arm. The number of patients with concurrent ALT or AST ≥ 3 x ULN and total bilirubin (BILI) ≥ 2 ULN was 1 in the Dato-DXd arm and 3 in the ICC arm. The one potential Hy's law case in the Dato-DXd arm was assessed as being due to the underlying cancer disease, which is agreed. The liver test elevations seen is similar to those reported earlier for Dato-DXd.

## Amylase increase

Shifts in clinical chemistry values were mainly 1-grade shifts, with only a small percentage of participants in the Dato-DXd and ICC arms developing a 3-grade or 4-grade shift, with the exception of amylase. Of the 224 patients in the Dato-DXd arm, shifts in amylase to grade 3 were observed for 46 (20.4%) and shifts to grade 4 were seen for 38 (16.9%), but none in the ICC arm. Fifty (15.7%) participants in the Dato-DXd arm and 6 (1.9%) participants in the ICC arm had an AE of amylase increase. There were no SAEs due to amylase increase. Of note, 2 AEs relating to pancreatitis were reported, both in the Dato-DXd arm, neither of which was considered by the investigator to be related to study intervention: these were SAEs of Grade 4 pancreatitis and Grade 3 obstructive pancreatitis, both of which resolved. Amylase increase led to Dato-DXd discontinuation in 1 (0.3%) patient and dose reduction in 22 (6.9%). Amylase increased was identified as a new ADR for Dato-DXd and has been added in the table of adverse reactions in SmPC section 4.8.

## Safety in special populations

Across the subgroups of age, renal and hepatic function, ECOG PS and baseline brain metastases, the safety profile of Dato-DXd was generally consistent with that of the rest of the Dato-DXd treated population. The subgroups were small, and the small differences in frequency of TEAEs are not considered significant. Importantly, there were no subgroups where interruptions, dose reductions or discontinuations were remarkably different from the rest of the Dato-DXd treated study population.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Discontinuations

AEs leading to discontinuation occurred in 4.7% in the Dato-DXd arm and 7.8% in the ICC arm. All AEs leading to discontinuation of study treatment in both treatment arms were reported in 1 participant each, except pneumonitis/ILD and keratitis in the Dato-DXd arm (5 [1.5%], and 2 [0.6%] participants, respectively) and neuropathy peripheral, anaemia, nausea, and paraesthesia in the ICC arm (7 [2.3%], 2 [0.6%], 2 [0.6%], and 2 [0.6%], respectively).

Overall, the data presented illustrate a toxicity profile of Dato-DXd, characterized by stomatitis, amylase increase and eye disorders, whereas the safety profile of the chemotherapy is mainly characterized by haematological toxicity.

## 2.5.2. Conclusions on clinical safety

In the pivotal study TB02, the safety profile of datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) was compared to investigator's choice of chemotherapy (ICC), i.e. taxanes, capecitabine, eribulin or carboplatin, in the first line setting of unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer. Supportive safety data from 1365 patients, of which 841 are patients with breast cancer, was also provided, which showed no clinically significant changes of the known safety profile nor any new safety findings. While the frequency of AEs and SAEs was similar in the Dato-DXd arm and the ICC arm of the TB02 study, the nature of the safety profiles differed, with stomatitis and ocular surface events being characteristic for Dato-DXd and haematological events dominating the chemotherapy safety profile. Nausea and vomiting were seen in both arms, however twice as often in the Dato-DXd arm and appeared to be manageable. ILD/pneumonitis is still the greatest risk in the treatment with Dato-DXd and one grade 3 event as reported by investigator was adjudicated as a fatal ILD in the TB02 study. This risk is appropriately reflected in the SmPC and the RMP. In the targeted setting, the observed toxicities of Dato-DXd are considered acceptable and manageable.

## 2.5.3. PSUR cycle

The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the list of Union reference dates (EURD list) provided for under Article 107c(7) of Directive 2001/83/EC and any subsequent updates published on the European medicines web-portal.

## 2.7.1. User consultation

No full user consultation with target patient groups on the package leaflet has been performed on the basis of a bridging report making reference to Enhertu. The bridging report submitted by the MAH has been found acceptable.

## 2.7.2. Additional monitoring

Pursuant to Article 23(1) of Regulation No (EU) 726/2004, Datroway (datopotamab deruxtecan) is included in the additional monitoring list as it contains a new active substance which, on 1 January 2011, was not contained in any medicinal product authorised in the EU.

Therefore, the summary of product characteristics and the package leaflet includes a statement that this medicinal product is subject to additional monitoring and that this will allow quick identification of new safety information. The statement is preceded by an inverted equilateral black triangle.

## 3. Benefit-Risk Balance

## 3.1. Therapeutic Context

## 3.1.1. Disease or condition

This is an extension of indication for datopotamab deruxtecan (Dato-DXd) as monotherapy for the first-line treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

## 3.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

Metastatic hormone-receptor negative and HER2-negative breast cancer, commonly referred to as \"triple-negative\" breast cancer (TNBC) remains an uncurable disease. Single-agent chemotherapy remains the mainstay of first-line treatment for these patients, with the addition of checkpoint inhibitors for patients with PD-L1 positive tumours, while PARP inhibitors may be considered for those patients with germline BRCA mutations (Gradishar et al 2024, Gennari et al 2021, Cardoso et al 2024).

For patients with TNBC who are ineligible for checkpoint inhibitors, which is the targeted patient population in this procedure, there are only chemotherapy options in the first line setting. The median PFS for patients treated with first-line chemotherapy for either recurrent or de novo metastatic TNBC is approximately 5.5 months, with median OS less than 2 years (Cortes et al 2020, Emens et al 2021, Miles et al 2021).

After first-line chemotherapy, these patients have the option of Sacituzumab govitecan, which is an anti-TROP2 ADC recommended for the treatment of adult patients with metastatic TNBC who have received 2 or more prior systemic therapies, at least one of them for advanced disease, but it is not recommended in the first-line setting (Gennari et al 2021, Gradishar et al 2024). Finally, patients with HER2-low or ultralow expressing tumours (IHC 1+ or IHC 2+/ISH-, or HER2-ultralow tumour status, described as IHC 0 with membrane staining (IHC &gt;0&lt;1+)) have the option of trastuzumab deruxtecan in the metastatic setting after having previously received chemotherapy after disease progression.

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.1.3. Main clinical studies

TROPION-Breast02 (TB02) is a phase III, randomized, open-label, 2-arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with investigator's choice of chemotherapy (ICC) in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy.

The TB02 trial randomised 644 patients in a 1:1 design and the dual primary efficacy endpoints were PFS by BICR and OS.

## 3.2. Favourable effects

At the final analysis on DCO 25 august 2025, the median PFS by BICR was 10.84 months (95% CI: 8.57, 12.98) in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 5.55 months (95% CI: 4.96, 6.97) in the ICC arm with a HR of 0.57 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.69).

At the final analysis on DCO 25 august 2025, the median OS was 23.66 months (95% CI: 19.81, 25.63) in the Dato-DXd arm vs 18.66 months (95% CI: 15.97, 21.78) in the ICC arm with a HR of 0.79 (95% CI: 0.64, 0.98).

OS HRs for Region 1 (US/Canada/Europe) and European population were 1.22 (95% CI: 0.86, 1.73) and 1.34 (95% CI: 0.90, 1.99), respectively. A multivariate Cox proportional hazards model OS sensitivity analysis adjusting for imbalanced baseline prognostic characteristics demonstrated OS HRs of 1.02 (95% CI: 0.71, 1.48) and 1.11 (0.73, 1.69) for Region 1 and the European population, respectively. In Region 1, subsequent ADC use occurred in 50.8% compared to 20.9% in Region 2 (Rest of World). A two-stage OS sensitivity analysis adjusting for ADC use only, demonstrated OS HRs of 0.83 (95% CI: 0.59, 1.18) and 0.79 (0.53, 1.18) for Region 1 and the European population, respectively.

Median PFS by investigator was 9.63 months (95% CI: 7.36, 11.20) in the Dato-DXd arm compared to 5.22 months (95% CI: 4.21, 5.59) in the ICC arm with a corresponding HR of 0.56 (95% CI: 0.47, 0.67).

## 3.3. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

Efficacy within Region 1 (US/Canada/Europe) and European population in terms of OS was not so pronounced compared to the ITT. Apart from imbalance of prognostic factors at baseline, there were regional imbalances in the use of subsequent ADC therapies control arm. Adjusted models of OS alleviated part of the uncertainties and reassured of a lack of detrimental effect.

## 3.4. Unfavourable effects

In the Dato-DXd treated subjects of the pivotal TB02 study:

- Any TEAE occurred in 98.7%
- Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs occurred in 43.3%
- SAEs occurred in 16.9%
- The most commonly observed ADRs were stomatitis (58.3%), nausea (48.3%), alopecia (42.9%), constipation (39.8%) and dry eye (26.3%), vomiting (22.6%), anaemia (20.7%), decreased appetite (19.1%), cough (18.5%), fatigue (17.6%), asthenia (15.4%), keratitis (13.8%) and rash (12.9%).

\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_\\_

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- AESIs are stomatitis (58.3%), ocular surface events(51.1%) and ILD/pneumonitis (3.8%).
- ILD/pneumonitis is the main safety concern, as fatal outcomes have been observed.

## 3.5. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

None.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 3.7. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 3.7.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

The efficacy of Dato-DXd in metastatic TNBC is investigated in the pivotal randomised, open label TB02 trial, where Dato-DXd was compared to the current standard of care treatment of singleagent chemotherapy per clinician's choice (ICC). Primary dual endpoints of PFS by BICR and OS showed statistically and clinically relevant benefits of Dato-DXd over standard single-agent chemotherapy in the first-line treatment of unresectable and metastatic TNBC with compelling magnitudes of benefits in PFS (5.29 months longer, HR 0.57) and OS (5.00 months longer, HR 0.79). The data is considered mature with 63.4% maturity for PFS and 54.2% maturity for OS. The dual primary efficacy outcomes were supported by clinically relevant benefits in other secondary outcomes, such as PFS by investigator (4.11 months longer) and ORR/DoR.

The Applicant has clarified that no additional OS data was collected after the FA DCO. This is also stated in the end of study definitions from the initial study protocol dated 21. November 2021.

Uncertainties were identified regarding the OS outcomes in the Region 1 and the European population. The variability seen in the OS measures between regions suggest that the extent of the overall impact of Dato-DXd on overall survival in breast cancer may depend on the other treatments available and the evolving treatment paradigm. Although the benefit does not appear as pronounced as in the ITT population, adjusted models of OS alleviated part of the uncertainties and reassured of a lack of detrimental effect.

No clinically significant changes of the known safety profile, nor any new safety findings were detected. While the frequency of AEs and SAEs was similar in the Dato-DXd arm and the ICC arm of the TB02 study, the nature of the safety profile differed clinically significantly, with stomatitis and ocular surface eventsbeing characteristic for Dato-DXd and haematological events dominating the chemotherapy safety profile. Nausea and vomiting were seen in both arms, however twice as often in the Dato-DXd arm and appeared to be manageable. ILD/pneumonitis is still the greatest risk in the treatment with Dato-DXd and one fatal event was reported in the TB02 study. In the targeted setting of unresectable or metastatic TNBC, the observed toxicities of Dato-DXd are considered acceptable and manageable.

## 3.7.2. Balance of benefits and risks

## 3.7.3. Additional considerations on the benefit-risk balance

Not applicable.

## 3.8. Conclusions

The overall B/R of Datroway is positive.

Extension of indication to include, as monotherapy, the first-line treatment of adult patients with unresectable or metastatic triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) who are not candidates for PD1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy for DATROWAY, based on final results from study D926PC00001 (TROPION-Breast02). This is a Phase 3, randomised, open-label, 2 arm, multicentre, international study assessing the efficacy and safety of Dato-DXd compared with investigator's choice chemotherapy in participants with locally recurrent inoperable or metastatic TNBC who are not candidates for PD-1/PD-L1 inhibitor therapy. As a consequence, sections 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.6, 4.8, 5.1 and 5.2 of the SmPC are updated. The Package Leaflet is updated in accordance. Version 2.0 of the RMP has also been submitted. In addition, the MAH took the opportunity to introduce minor changes to the PI.

## Amendments to the marketing authorisation

In view of the data submitted with the variation, amendments to Annexes I, II, and IIIB and to the Risk Management Plan are recommended.

## Conditions or restrictions with regard to the safe and effective use of the medicinal product

## Risk management plan (RMP)

The MAH shall perform the required pharmacovigilance activities and interventions detailed in the agreed RMP presented in Module 1.8.2 of the Marketing Authorisation and any agreed subsequent updates of the RMP.

In addition, an updated RMP should be submitted:

At the request of the European Medicines Agency;

Whenever the risk management system is modified, especially as the result of new information being received that may lead to a significant change to the benefit/risk profile or as the result of an important (pharmacovigilance or risk minimisation) milestone being reached.

## Additional market protection

The MAH withdrew the request for consideration of its application in accordance with Article 14(11) of Regulation (EC) 726/2004 for 'one year of market protection for a new indication' while the procedure was ongoing.

## 5. EPAR changes

The EPAR will be updated following Commission Decision for this variation. In particular the \"EPARProcedural steps taken and scientific information after authorisation\" will be updated as follows:

Please refer to the Recommendations section above.
